We follow the approach of
[
14
]
to prove nontangential maximal function estimates, with some important modifications. The approach originated in
[
22
]
, with critical new developments in
[
12
]
and
[
6
]
. In

[
14
]
, we obtained bounds for the nontangential maximal function by the area integral and square function ( ‘
N<S+A
’ estimates) but only for parabolic operators in block form.
Key innovations here include: (1) removing the block-form assumption on the parabolic operators, (2) using the
p
-adapted square function
S_{p}
(rather than the square function) to derive ‘
N<S_{p}
’ estimates for both the tangential gradient and the ‘conormal derivative’ of solutions.

Let us begin by introducing some notions that will be used throughout the three subsections.
As we now work on just upper half-space, we define, as in
[
14
, Section 10]
, for a fixed background parameter

a>0
, the nontangential cone
\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)
for a boundary point
(q,\tau)\in\partial(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})
as follows:

(5.1)

\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)=\{(X,t)=(x,x_{n},t):\,x_{n}>a^{-1}\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|\}.

The cones (
5.1
) are better for our purposes here, as the special direction
x_{n}
is separated from all other variables. We will refer to the parameter
a
, as before, as the aperture, and

a^{-1}
we shall call the slope of the nontangential cone
\gamma_{a}
. For the remainder of this section we assume that
N
and
S

are defined with respect to the cones
\gamma_{a}
.

These cones, as defined currently, have vertices at the boundary, but in order to define the stopping time construction we also need to define interior cones (with vertices inside
\Omega
). In the elliptic setting these interior cones
are defined by simply shifting the boundary cone, that is for
(Q,\tau)=(q,q_{n},\tau)\in\Omega

(5.2)

\gamma_{a}(q,q_{n},\tau)=(0,q_{n},0)+\{(X,t)=(x,x_{n},t):\,x_{n}>a^{-1}\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|\}.

This is the approach also taken in
[
29
]
. However, we prefer to modify this construction and consider “enlarged” cones that have an additional feature which we explain below. These novel cones were introduced in
[
14
]
.

Firstly, let us consider the set
S(X,t)=\{(q,\tau)\in\partial(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}):\,(X,t)\in\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)\}
. Given the way the cones are defined, it follows that

(5.3)

S(X,t)=\{(q,\tau):\,\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|<a\,x_{n}\},

which is an open parabolic ball centered at
(x,t)
of “radius”
a\,x_{n}
. We define the cone
\gamma_{a}
at
(X,t)
as the interior of the intersection of all boundary cones that originate in
S(X,t)
. Hence

(5.4)

\gamma_{a}(X,t)=\gamma_{a}(x,x_{n},t)=\mbox{int}\bigcap\{\gamma_{a}(q,\tau):\,\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|<a\,x_{n}\}.

To explain this definition we compare (
5.2
) and (
5.4
). When the time variable is not present there is no difference between these definitions, and thus there is no reason to consider (
5.4
). This is due to the fact that in this case the boundary of the cone (
5.2
) is a union of straight lines of slope
a^{-1}
originating from its vertex and intersections of such cones results in a cone of the same type.

This is not the case with the time variable. In this variable the cone defined by (
5.2
) has a cusp of shape
given by the curve
a^{-1}\sqrt{|t|}
at zero.
The definition (
5.4
) removes this cusp for interior cones at
x_{n}>0
; it is easy to see that the boundary cones

\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)
that define
\gamma_{a}(X,t)
have bounded slope of their boundary in
t
-variable at height
x_{n}>0
. This slope is bounded by
\frac{a^{-2}}{2x_{n}}
(this is derived from the slope of the function
a^{-1}\sqrt{|t|}
).

Thus it follows that the boundary of the cone
\gamma_{a}(X,t)
is given by the graph of a function that is Lipschitz
with Lipschitz constant
a^{-1}
in spatial variables and Lipschitz constant
\frac{a^{-2}}{2x_{n}}
in the time variable.

Moreover, our new cones (
5.4
) maintain a key property of the cones from (
5.2
), namely, that
if
(Q,\tau)
is any point (including a boundary point) and
(X,t)\in\gamma_{a}(Q,\tau)
then

\overline{\gamma_{a}(X,t)}\subset\gamma_{a}(Q,\tau).

As in
[
12
]
we consider the map
\hbar:\partial\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
given at each
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
defined by

(5.5)

\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t):=\inf\left\{x_{n}>0:\,\sup_{(Z,s)\in\gamma_{a}(x,x_{n},t)}w(Z,s)<\nu\right\}.

The function
w
is defined below in (
5.11
) and varies
depending on the exact form of the ‘
N<S_{p}
’ estimate. The properties of the cones are mainly used to derive some key properties of the function
\hbar
in Lemma
5.13
.

5.1.
N<S_{p}
for
u

The goal of this subsection is to prove Theorem
5.6
. We start by only considering
\mathcal{L}u=0
. In the next two subsections, we will adjust the proof to more general settings so that this can be applied to
\nabla_{T}u
and
H
. In the theorem below it might look strange that we consider energy solutions with given boundary data
f
(as the objective of this paper is to solve the Neumann problem), but for the purposes of this section our objective is to prove
N<S_{p}
for all solutions
u\in\dot{\mathsf{E}}(\Omega)
and all such solutions are generated by solving the Dirichlet problem with
f\in\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega)
.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
A
be a matrix with bounded measurable coefficients that satisfies the ellipticity condition, the Carleson condition for coefficients
(a_{nj})_{j=1,2,\dots,n}
. Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
.

Then there exists
\delta>0
such that if

\||\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}^{1/2}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}^{1/2}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

where
\mathbf{a_{n}}
is the
n
-th row of the matrix
A
, then for any
p
such that
p/2
is a positive even integer, for any
a>0
,
f\in L^{q}(\partial\Omega)\cap\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega)
, there exists a
constant
C=C(n,p,a,\delta,\lambda,\Lambda)>0
, such that for the energy solution
u
to
\mathcal{L}u=0
,
u\big|_{\partial\Omega}=f
, there holds for all
q>p/2

(5.7)

\|{\tilde{N}}(u)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C\left\|S_{p}(u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)},

provided we know a priori that
\|\tilde{N}(u)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
. Same claim also holds for solutions
u_{k}
on the domains
\Omega^{k}=\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times(0,k)\times\mathbb{R}
with boundary data
u_{k}(\cdot,0,\cdot)=f
and
u_{k}(\cdot,k,\cdot)=0
.

We introduce solutions
u_{k}
,
in order to gain a necessary decay as
x_{n}\to\infty
. Let

(5.8)

\mathcal{L}u_{k}=0\mbox{ on }\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times(0,k)\times\mathbb{R},\quad u_{k}(x,0,t)=f(x,t),\quad u_{k}(x,k,t)=0,\quad\mbox{for all }(x,t)\in\partial\Omega.

For simplicity we extend
u_{k}
to
\Omega
by defining
u_{k}(x,x_{n},t)=0
when
x_{n}>k
, which extends each
u_{k}
continuously.
We now consider the limit of
u_{k}
, as
k\to\infty
. For each
k\geq 1
,
the Lax-Milgram lemma used on the domain
\Omega^{k}=\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times(0,k)\times\mathbb{R}
gives us

(5.9)

\|u_{k}\|_{\dot{\mathsf{E}}}=\left(\|\nabla u_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|H_{t}D_{t}^{1/2}u_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\|\mbox{Tr }u_{k}\|_{\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega^{k})}=C\|f\|_{\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega)},

where the constant
C\in(0,\infty)
depends on the coercivity constant of a certain
sesquilinear form (see
[
14
]
, Section 2)
in
\Omega^{k}
(which is uniform in
k
), and
\|A\|_{L^{\infty}}=\Lambda
. Therefore, this
bound is uniform in
k
. This uniformity and the fact that
\text{Tr}(u_{k})=f
, implies that a weak convergence argument
yields a sub-sequence convergent to some
v
with
\|v\|_{\dot{\mathsf{E}}}\leq C\|f\|_{\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega)}

and
\text{Tr}(v)=f
. This sub-sequence (which we somewhat imprecisely also call
(u_{k})
) is therefore strongly convergent to
v
in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})

by a standard functional analysis argument.
It follows that the
L^{2}
averages of
u_{k}
converge locally and uniformly to
the
L^{2}
averages of
v
in
C_{\rm loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})
. Additionally, because each
u_{k}
has uniform interior Hölder continuity estimates, the convergence of
u_{k}\to v
also holds in
C^{\alpha}_{\rm loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})
for some
\alpha>0
.

Taking the limit in
k
, it now follows that
v
must be a weak solution to
\mathcal{L}v=0
in
\Omega
. Since
\text{Tr}(v)=f
and
\|v\|_{\dot{\mathsf{E}}}<\infty
, it must hold that
u=v
since energy solutions are unique. Consider now
u_{k}-u
, a solution in
\Omega^{k}
enjoying the bound

(5.10)

\|u_{k}-u\|_{\dot{\mathsf{E}}}\leq C\|\mbox{Tr }(u_{k}-u)\|_{\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega^{k})}=C\|\mbox{Tr }u\|_{\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\{k\}\times\mathbb{R})}.

The trace
\|\mbox{Tr }u\|_{\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\{k\}\times\mathbb{R})}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
, otherwise
\|u\|_{\dot{\mathsf{E}}}=\infty
, which is false. Thus the weak convergence
\nabla u_{k}\to\nabla u
in
L^{2}
upgrades to strong convergence.

We define
w
as the
L^{p}
average of
|u_{k}|
for some fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
, that is, for
p>1
,

(5.11)

w(X,t)=w(x,x_{n},t)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{x_{n}/2}(X,t)}|u_{k}(Y,s)|^{p}\,dY\,ds\right)^{1/p}.

At this point we observe that
\hbar_{\nu,a}w(x,t)<\infty
for all points
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
.
This is due to the fact that
u_{k}=0
for
x_{n}>k
and therefore
w=0
for
x_{n}>2k
.

For a constant
\nu>0
, define the set

(5.12)

E_{\nu,a}:=\big\{(x,t)\in\partial\Omega:\,N_{a}(w)(x,t)>\nu\big\}.

The following lemma is proved in
[
14
, Lemma 10.10]
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
w
be as in (
5.11
). Then the following properties hold.

(i)
The function
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)
is Lipschitz in spatial variables with constant
a^{-1}
. It is also
Lipschitz in time variable above a positive height and the following holds:

(5.14)

\left|\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t)-\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(y,s)\right|\leq a^{-1}|x-y|+\frac{a^{-2}}{2\min\{\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t),\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(y,s)\}}|t-s|.

for all
(x,t),(y,s)\in\partial\Omega
. In particular,

(5.15)

|\nabla\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t)|\leq a^{-1},\qquad|\partial_{t}\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t)|\leq\frac{a^{-2}}{2\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t)}.

(ii)
The function
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)
satisfies the following: there exists
C_{n}>0
that depends only on
n
such that

(5.16)

\left|\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t)-\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(y,s)\right|\leq C_{n}a^{-1}\left(\left|x-y\right|+\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}\right)

for all
(x,t),(y,s)\in\partial\Omega
.

(iii)
Given an arbitrary
(x,t)\in E_{\nu,a}
, where
E_{\nu,a}
is defined in (
5.12
), let
x_{n}:=\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t)
. Then there exists a
point
(y,y_{n},s)\in\partial\gamma_{a}(x,x_{n},t)
such that
w(y,y_{n},s)=\nu
and
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(y,s)=y_{n}
.

The following statement (for the square function
S_{p,b}
with
p=2
only) appears in
[
14
]
in Appendix A, where the matrices were also assumed to be in block form.

Lemma 5.17
.

Assume that
w
is defined as in (
5.11
) and
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Then for any
a>0
there exists
0<b_{0}=b_{0}(a)
and
\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{0}(a)>0
such that the following holds.
Having fixed an arbitrary
\nu>0
,
b\geq b_{0}
, and
\gamma\leq\gamma_{0}
for each point
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
from the set

(5.18)

\big\{(x,t):\,N_{a}(w)(x,t)>\nu\mbox{ and }S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu\big\}

there exists a boundary ball
R
with
(x,t)\in K_{0}R
,
where
K_{0}R
is an enlargement of
R
by a factor
K_{0}>1
that depends only on
p
, dimension, and the aperture
a
, and such that

(5.19)

\big|w\big(z,\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(z,\tau),\tau\big)\big|>\nu/{8}\,\,\text{ for all }\,\,(z,\tau)\in R.

Proof of Lemma
5.17
.

The statement follows by a modified argument from Appendix A of
[
14
]
adapted to our case.
For a point
(x,t)
in (
5.18
), part (iii) of Lemma
5.13
gives the existence of a point
(y,y_{n},s)\in\partial\gamma_{a}(x,x_{n},t)
on the graph of
\hbar
with
w(y,y_{n},s)=\nu
.
We consider an interior parabolic cylinder
Q_{y_{n}/2}(y,y_{n},s)
. There exists
b=b(a)>0
such that

Q_{4y_{n}/5}(y,y_{n},s)\subset\gamma_{b}(x,t)
and hence (
5.19
) will follow if we prove that for all points inside
Q_{\delta y_{n}}(y,y_{n},s)
the function
w
is at least
\nu/8
, for some
\delta>0
depending only on
p
.

Firstly,
w(y,y_{n},s)=\nu
implies that the
L^{p}
average of
|u_{k}|
over the cube
Q_{y_{n}/2}(y,y_{n},s)
is
\nu
. Denote by
\tilde{B}
the orthogonal projection of
Q_{cy_{n}/2}(y,y_{n},s)
, onto the spatial domain
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, where
c=c(p)
is a constant (larger than
1
and less than
8/5
) to be determined later. Denote by
B
the orthogonal projection of
Q_{y_{n}/2}(y,y_{n},s)
onto
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
. Let
\eta
be a smooth nonnegative cutoff
function in spatial variables, supported in
\tilde{B}
and satisfying
\int_{\tilde{B}}\eta^{2}\,dX=1
,
|\nabla\eta|\lesssim y_{n}^{-1}\eta
, and

\sup_{B}\eta^{2}\lesssim\fint_{\tilde{B}}\eta^{2}\,dX\lesssim\inf_{B}\eta^{2}
.
Consider the function

v_{av}(\tau):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|u_{k}|^{p}(X,\tau)\eta^{2}(X)\,dX.

With the properties of
\eta
and the fact that

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{y_{n}/2}(y,y_{n},s)}|u_{k}|^{p}dX\,dt=\nu^{p},

we must have

(5.20)

v_{av}(\tau)>(\nu/2)^{p}\text{ for some }\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s):=[s-(y_{n})^{2}/2,s+(y_{n})^{2}/2],

because otherwise one would have

y_{n}^{2}\left(\nu/2\right)^{p}\geq\int_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)d\tau\geq\left(\inf_{B}\eta^{2}\right)\iint_{Q_{y_{n}/2}(Y,s)}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p}dXd\tau\geq\frac{y_{n}^{2}|B|}{2|\tilde{B}|}\nu^{p}\geq c^{n}\frac{y_{n}^{2}}{2}\nu^{p},

which is a contradiction if
p>1
and
c=c(p)
is chosen sufficiently close to
1
.
We claim that (
5.20
) implies that

(5.21)

v_{av}(\tau^{\prime})>(\nu/4)^{p}\text{ for all }\tau^{\prime}\in I_{y_{n}}(s).

Indeed, calculating as in
[
8
]
, we multiply the equation
\mathcal{L}u=0
by
\eta^{2}
and integrate it over the region

\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]
for any
t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}\in I_{y_{n}}(s)
, extending the function
u_{k}\eta
by zero outside the support of
\eta
.
Because
\eta
is a
function of the spatial variables only, we have

(5.22)

v_{av}(t_{0}^{\prime})-v_{av}(t_{0})=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\{t_{0}^{\prime}\}}|u_{k}|^{p}\eta^{2}\,dX-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\{t_{0}\}}|u_{k}|^{p}\eta^{2}\,dX\\
=p\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(\partial_{t}u_{k})|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\,\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt=p\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}\mbox{\rm div}(A\nabla u_{k})|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\,\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt\\
=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}\mbox{\rm div}(A\nabla(|u_{k}|^{p}))\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt-p(p-1)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k})|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt\\
=-2p\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla\eta)|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\eta\,dX\,dt\\
-p(p-1)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k})|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt.

The term
|\nabla\eta|
on the right-hand side integral is bounded by

\eta y_{n}^{-1}
, and the length of the interval
[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]
is bounded by
y_{n}^{2}
.
So by Cauchy-Schwarz and the properties of
\eta
,

(5.23)

\left|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla\eta)|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\eta\,dX\,dt\right|\\
\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times I_{y_{n}}(s)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p-2}\eta^{2}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times I_{y_{n}}(s)}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p}\eta^{2}y_{n}^{-2}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\lesssim\left(\iint_{Q_{\frac{4y_{n}}{5}}(y,y_{n},s)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p-2}y_{n}^{-n}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\{\tau\}}|u_{k}|^{p}\eta^{2}\,dX\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq S_{p,b}(u_{k})^{p/2}(x,t)\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)^{1/2}\leq\frac{1}{4p}\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)+CS_{p,b}(u_{k})^{p}(x,t).

Therefore, we have that for any
t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}\in I_{y_{n}}(s)
,

\left|v_{av}(t_{0}^{\prime})-v_{av}(t_{0})\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)+CS_{p,b}(u_{k})^{p}(x,t).

Since
S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu
and
\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)\geq(\nu/2)^{p}
, the inequality above implies that

\inf_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)\geq\left(2^{-p-1}-C\gamma^{p}\right)\nu^{p}\geq\left(\frac{\nu}{4}\right)^{p}

must hold for sufficiently small
\gamma>0
. Hence we have shown the claim (
5.21
).

Pick now any point
(X_{0},t_{0})\in Q_{\delta y_{n}}(y,y_{n},s)
where

\delta=\frac{c(p)-1}{3}>0
. Denote by
B_{X_{0}}
the orthogonal projection of
Q_{x_{n}/2}(X_{0},t_{0})
onto the spatial domain
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
. We write
w^{p}(X_{0},t_{0})
as

(5.24)

w^{p}(X_{0},t_{0})=\fint_{s^{\prime}=t-(x_{n}/2)^{2}}^{t+(x_{n}/2)^{2}}\fint_{B_{X_{0}}}|u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})|^{p}\,dX\,ds^{\prime}=:\fint_{t-(x_{n}/2)^{2}}^{t+(x_{n}/2)^{2}}w_{av}(s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}.

We aim to obtain a lower bound for
w(X_{0},t_{0})^{p}
by comparing
w_{av}(s^{\prime})
with
v_{av}(s^{\prime})
. In fact, it is easier to compare the averages without the cutoff function
\eta
and we will control

\fint_{\tilde{B}}|u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})|^{p}dX-w_{av}(s^{\prime})=\fint_{\tilde{B}}|u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})|^{p}dX-\fint_{B_{X_{0}}}|u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})|^{p}\,dX.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus in the spatial variables (c.f.
[
12
, Lemma 5.2]
), we obtain that

\fint_{\tilde{B}}|u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})|^{p}dX-w_{av}(s^{\prime})\leq C\int_{X\in H}|u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})|^{p-1}\left|\nabla u_{k}(X,s^{\prime})\right|y_{n}^{1-n}dX,

where
H
is the convex hull of the set
\tilde{B}\cup B_{X_{0}}
in the spatial domain. Note that our choice of
X_{0}
,
\delta
and
\tilde{B}
guarantees that
B_{X_{0}}\subset\tilde{B}
, and thus
H=\tilde{B}
. It follows from Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequality that

\fint_{\tilde{B}\times\left\{s^{\prime}\right\}}|u_{k}|^{p}dX-w_{av}(s^{\prime})\leq C_{\varepsilon}\,y_{n}^{2}\int_{\tilde{B}\times\{s^{\prime}\}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p-2}y_{n}^{-n}dX+\varepsilon\int_{\tilde{B}\times\left\{s^{\prime}\right\}}|u_{k}|^{p}y_{n}^{-n}dX\\
\leq C\,y_{n}^{2}\int_{\tilde{B}\times\{s^{\prime}\}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p-2}y_{n}^{-n}dX+\frac{1}{2}\fint_{\tilde{B}\times\left\{s^{\prime}\right\}}|u_{k}|^{p}dX

by choosing
\varepsilon
sufficiently small. Reordering the terms gives that

w_{av}(s^{\prime})\geq\frac{1}{2}\fint_{\tilde{B}\times\left\{s^{\prime}\right\}}|u_{k}|^{p}dX-C\,y_{n}^{2}\int_{\tilde{B}\times\{s^{\prime}\}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}\right|^{p-2}y_{n}^{-n}dX.

Integrating both sides in
s^{\prime}\in[t_{0}-(x_{n}/2)^{2},t_{0}+(x_{n}/2)^{2}]
and taking averages, one gets that

(5.25)

\fint_{t-(x_{n}/2)^{2}}^{t+(x_{n}/2)^{2}}w_{av}(s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}\geq\frac{1}{2}\fint_{t-(x_{n}/2)^{2}}^{t+(x_{n}/2)^{2}}\fint_{\tilde{B}\times\left\{s^{\prime}\right\}}|u_{k}|^{p}dXds^{\prime}-CS_{p,b}(u)(x,t)^{p},

where we have used
x_{n}\approx y_{n}
. The property of
\eta
ensures that
v_{av}(s^{\prime})\leq\fint_{\tilde{B}\times\left\{s^{\prime}\right\}}|u_{k}|^{p}dX
for all
s^{\prime}
. Thus, (
5.25
), the claim (
5.21
) (notice that the interval
[t_{0}-(x_{n}/2)^{2},t_{0}+(x_{n}/2)^{2}]
is contained in
I_{y_{n}}(s)
), and the bound on
S_{p,b}(u)(x,t)
imply that

w^{p}(X_{0},t_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}(\nu/4)^{p}-C\gamma^{p}\nu^{p}\geq(\nu/8)^{p}

for
\gamma>0
sufficiently small.
Hence we have proved that on
Q_{\delta y_{n}}(y,y_{n},s)
the function
w
is at least
\nu/8
.
∎

Corollary 5.26
.

Let
w
be defined as in (
5.11
),
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
and fix
a>0
.
Associated with these, let
b_{0},\gamma_{0}
be as in Lemma
5.17
. Then there exists a finite
constant
C=C(n,p,a)>0
with the property that for any
\nu>0
,
b\geq b_{0}
,
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0}]
, and any point
(x,t)\in E_{\nu,a}

such that
S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu
, one has

(5.27)

(M_{\hbar_{\nu,a}}w)\big(x,\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t),t\big)\geq\,C\nu,

where
M_{\hbar_{\nu,a}}
is the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function on the graph of
\hbar_{\nu,a}
.

Finally, we are ready to state the key lemma that allows us to formulate a good-
\lambda
inequality later.
The calculation here is based on
[
14
]
with the necessary modifications taking into account the use of the
p
-adapted square function instead of the more usual
L^{2}
based square function.

Lemma 5.28
.

Fix
p
such that
p/2
is an even positive integer.
Let
\Omega={\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
and let
{\mathcal{L}}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
be a parabolic operator.
Suppose
\mathcal{L}u_{k}=0
is a weak solution in
\Omega^{k}
satisfying
u_{k}(x,k,t)=0
, extended to
\Omega
by setting
u_{k}=0
for
x_{n}>k
. For a fixed (sufficiently large)
a>0
determined below,, consider an arbitrary function
\hbar:{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that it satisfies
the estimates (
5.14
), (
5.15
) and
\hbar\geq 0
.
Then
we have the following:

For any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0

such that if

\||\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

where
\mathbf{a_{n}}
is the
n
-th row of the matrix
A
,
and
|\nabla A|x_{n}
is bounded, then
for all arbitrary parabolic surface balls
\Delta_{r}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
of radius
r
such that at least one point of
\Delta_{r}

satisfies
\hbar(x,t)\leq 2r
, we have the following estimate for an arbitrary
c\geq 0
:

(5.29)

\int_{1/6}^{6}\int_{\Delta_{r}}\big|u^{p/2}_{k}\big(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t\big)-c\big|^{2}\,dx\,dt\,d\theta\leq C(1+\varepsilon^{-1})\iint_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\\
+\varepsilon\left(\|\tilde{N}_{2,a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+\|{N}_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}\right)\\
+\varepsilon\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(y,s)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}^{-n}\right)^{p/2}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt+\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt,

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
that only depends on
a,\Lambda,n
but not on
k
,
u_{k}
,
c
,
\varepsilon
or
\Delta_{r}
.
Here,

\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar):=\left\{(x,x_{n},t):\,(x,t)\in\Delta_{2r}\text{ and }\frac{\hbar(x,t)}{12}<x_{n}<18r\right\},

where
\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)\cap\left\{(x,x_{n},t):x_{n}>\varepsilon r\right\}
.
The cones used to define the nontangential
maximal functions in this lemma have vertices on
\partial\Omega
.

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{r}
be as in the statement of our Lemma, and assume that
(q,\tau)
is the center of
\Delta_{r}
. Let
\zeta
be a smooth cutoff function of the form
\zeta(x,x_{n},t)=\zeta_{0}(x_{n})\zeta_{1}(x,t)
where

(5.30)

\zeta_{0}=\begin{cases}1&\text{ in }(-\infty,r_{0}+r],\\
0&\text{ in }[r_{0}+2r,\infty),\end{cases}\qquad\zeta_{1}=\begin{cases}1&\text{ in }\Delta_{r}(q,\tau),\\
0&\text{ in }\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)\end{cases}

and

(5.31)

r|\partial_{x_{n}}\zeta_{0}|+r|\nabla_{x}\zeta_{1}|+r^{2}|\partial_{t}\zeta_{1}|\leq c

for some constant
c\in(0,\infty)
independent of
r
. Here

r_{0}=6\sup_{(x,t)\in\Delta_{r}}\hbar(x,t)
. Observe that
r_{0}\leq(12+C_{n}a)r
with
C_{n}
as in (
5.16
), and so for
a
sufficiently large, one has
r_{0}\leq 13r
and

\theta\hbar(x,t)\leq r_{0}+r\qquad\mbox{for all }(x,t)\in\Delta_{2r},\text{ for }\theta\in(1/6,6).

Our goal is to control the
L^{2}
norm of
u_{k}^{p/2}\big(\cdot,\theta\hbar(\cdot)\big)-c
on
\Delta_{r}
. We proceed to estimate

\displaystyle\hskip-14.45377pt\int_{\Delta_{r}(q,\tau)}(u_{k}^{p/2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)-c)^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq\mathcal{I}:=\int_{\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)}(u_{k}^{p/2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)-c)^{2}\zeta(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 50.58878pt=-\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{x_{n}}\left[(u_{k}^{p/2}(x,x_{n},t)-c)^{2}\zeta(x,x_{n},t)\right]\,dx_{n}\,dx\,dt,

where
\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)=\{(x,x_{n},t):(x,t)\in\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)\mbox{ and }\theta\hbar(x,t)<x_{n}<r_{0}+2r\}
. Hence:

\displaystyle\hskip 7.22743pt\mathcal{I}\leq-2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\zeta\,dx_{n}\,\,dt\,dx

(5.32)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.58878pt\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}(x,x_{n},t)\partial_{x_{n}}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dx\,dt=:\mathcal{A}+V.

We further expand the term
\mathcal{A}
as a sum of three terms obtained
via integration by parts with respect to
x_{n}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=-2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)(\partial_{x_{n}}x_{n})\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle=2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\left|\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial^{2}_{x_{n}x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)(\partial_{x_{n}}u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{\mathcal{\partial}S(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\zeta\,\nu_{n}dS

(5.33)

\displaystyle=:I+II+III+IV.

Here the boundary term
IV
is nonvanishing only on the graph of function
\theta\hbar
.

We start by analyzing the term
II
. As each
u_{k}
solves the PDE in
\Omega^{k}
and equals zero at
x_{n}=k
,
we can if necessary reflect
u_{k}
across the
x_{n}=k
boundary so that it solves the parabolic PDE even above
x_{n}=k
. Then we have for
u_{k}^{p/2}-c
:

{\mathcal{L}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)=\frac{p}{2}u_{k}^{p/2-1}\left[-\partial_{t}u_{k}+\divg(A\nabla u_{k})\right]+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)u_{k}^{p/2-2}A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k}.

As the first term equals zero (since
u_{k}
is a solution to
\mathcal{L}
) we are left with

{\mathcal{L}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)=\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)u_{k}^{p/2-2}A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k}>0.

It follows that we can write

(5.34)

\partial^{2}_{x_{n}x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)=\frac{1}{a_{nn}}\partial_{x_{n}}(a_{nn}\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2}))-\frac{\partial_{x_{n}}a_{nn}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})=\\
\frac{1}{a_{nn}}\left[\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})-\sum_{(i,j)\neq(n,n)}\partial_{x_{i}}(a_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2}))+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)u_{k}^{p/2-2}A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k}-\partial_{x_{n}}a_{nn}\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right].

In turn, this permits us to write the term
II
as

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{(i,j)\neq(n,n)}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{1}{a_{nn}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{{x_{i}}}\left({a}_{ij}\partial_{{x_{j}}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right)x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\frac{\partial_{x_{n}}a_{nn}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+p\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)u_{k}^{p/2-2}A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k}\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

(5.35)

\displaystyle\quad+2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{1}{a_{nn}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx=\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{C}+\mathcal{D}+II_{7}.

We leave the analysis of the last term
II_{7}
for later. Observe that, under the assumption of the lemma
c\geq 0
, and using non-negativity of
u_{k}^{p/2-2}
we get for the term
\mathcal{D}
:

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}

\displaystyle\leq p\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}u_{k}^{p-2}A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k}\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|u_{k}|^{p-2}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx.

For the term
\mathcal{B}
we integrate by parts w.r.t.
\partial_{x_{i}}
, provided
i<n
calling the resulting terms
\mathcal{B}_{i}
. When
i=n
we must have
j<n
and we then swap the two derivatives since

\partial_{x_{n}}\left({a}_{nj}\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right)=\partial_{{x_{j}}}\left({a}_{nj}\partial_{{x_{n}}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right)+\partial_{x_{n}}(a_{nj})\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2})-\partial_{x_{j}}(a_{nj})\partial_{{x_{n}}}(u_{k}^{p/2}),

and subsequently we can integrate by parts w.r.t.
\partial_{x_{j}}
instead. (We denote the resulting terms
\mathcal{B}_{j}
). Notice that the last two terms in the formula above are of a type similar to
\mathcal{C}
, and we denote those by
\mathcal{B}_{res1}
.

This then yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{i}+\mathcal{B}_{j}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i<n}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{a_{ij}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{x_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+2\sum_{j<n}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle-2\sum_{i<n}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{a_{ij}\partial_{x_{i}}a_{nn}}{a_{nn}^{2}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

(5.37)

\displaystyle-2\sum_{j<n}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{a_{nj}\partial_{x_{j}}a_{nn}}{a_{nn}^{2}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+2\sum_{i<n}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{a_{ij}}{a_{nn}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{j}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+2\sum_{j<n}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,x_{n}\partial_{x_{j}}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-2\sum_{i<n}\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(\mbox{boundary terms})x_{n}\zeta\nu_{i}\,dS

\displaystyle=:II_{1}+II_{2}+\mathcal{B}_{res2}+\mathcal{B}_{res3}+II_{3}+II_{4}+II_{5}.

The boundary integral (term
II_{5}
) vanishes everywhere except on the graph of the function
\theta\hbar
. Note that on the graph of
\theta\hbar
,

(5.38)

\nu_{i}=\frac{-\theta\partial_{i}\hbar}{J_{h}}dx\,dt,\quad dS=J_{h}\,dx\,dt\quad\text{for }i=1,\dots,n-1,

where
J_{h}:=\left(1+(\theta\nabla_{x}\hbar)^{2}+(\theta\partial_{t}\hbar)^{2}\right)^{1/2}
, and so

\left|\nu_{i}\right|dS\leq\theta\left|\partial_{i}\hbar\right|dx\,dt\leq\theta\,a^{-1}dx\,dt
. Hence,

\displaystyle|II_{5}|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i<n}\int_{\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)}|(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)\nabla_{x}(u_{k}^{p/2})(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)\hbar(x,t)\zeta(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)|dt\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)}(u_{k}^{p/2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)-c)^{2}\zeta(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)}|\nabla_{x}(u_{k}^{p/2})(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)|^{2}|\hbar(x,t)|^{2}\,dt\,dx=\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{I}+II_{6}.

In the last step we used Cauchy-Schwarz. We note that an analogous estimate also applies to the term
IV
.
We will hide the term
\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{I}
on the left-hand side of (
5.32
), while the second term becomes, after integrating
II_{6}
in
\theta
:

\displaystyle\int_{1/6}^{6}|II_{6}|\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{1/6}^{6}\int_{\Delta_{2r}(q,\tau)}|\nabla(u_{k}^{p/2})(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)|^{2}|\hbar(x,t)|^{2}dt\,dxd\theta.

(5.39)

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dt\,dxdx_{n},

which is an estimate similar to that obtained for
\mathcal{D}
. Observe also that the terms
I
,
II_{1}
and
II_{2}
also enjoy a similar estimate which we record as:

(5.40)

|I+II_{1}+II_{2}|\leq C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx.

Next, we handle the terms
\mathcal{B}_{res1}
-
\mathcal{B}_{res3}
and
\mathcal{C}
. The Carleson condition for
|\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}|^{2}x_{n}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality imply

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{res1}+\mathcal{B}_{res2}+\mathcal{B}_{res3}+\mathcal{C}|\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}||u_{k}^{p/2}-c||\nabla_{x}(u_{k}^{p/2})|\,x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}^{p/2}-c\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\left|\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}\right|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}^{1/2}\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}\cdot\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}

(5.41)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx.

Next, since
r|\nabla\zeta|\leq c
, if the derivative falls on the cutoff function
\zeta
we have

\displaystyle|II_{3}+II_{4}+III|\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla_{x}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right||u_{k}^{p/2}-c|\frac{x_{n}}{r}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\frac{x_{n}}{r^{2}}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}^{p-2}|x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

By Fubini’s theorem, and the fact that

\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}y_{n}^{-n-1}dydsdy_{n}\leq Cr
, the first integral on the right-hand side can be controlled by the nontangential maximal function:

(5.42)

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\frac{x_{n}}{r^{2}}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx\leq\frac{C}{r}\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|u_{k}^{p/2}-c\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n-1}dydsdy_{n}\\
\leq C\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\tilde{N}_{a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)^{2}dx\,dt.

Hence, once again

(5.43)

\left|II_{3}+II_{4}+III\right|\leq\\
\varepsilon\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx.

In a similar spirit we analyze the term
II_{7}
. We write it as

(5.44)

\displaystyle II_{7}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{t}[a_{nn}^{-1}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}]x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}x_{n}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}a_{nn}^{-1}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}x_{n}(\partial_{t}\zeta)\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(\mbox{boundary terms})x_{n}\zeta\nu_{t}\,dS=:II_{8}+II_{9}+II_{10},

where the boundary terms only arise on the portion of
\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)
where

\zeta
is supported (which means on the graph of the function
\theta\hbar
).
\nu_{t}
denotes the co-normal
in the
t
-direction.
Note that on the graph of
\theta\hbar
,
\nu_{t}=-\frac{\partial_{t}\hbar}{J_{h}}
and
dS=J_{h}dx\,dt
, where
J_{h}
is defined above the computation for
II_{5}
. By (
5.15
), we have that
\left|\nu_{t}\right|dS\leq\frac{a^{-2}}{2x_{n}}dx\,dt
.
It follows that

|II_{10}|\lesssim\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\int_{\Delta(q,\tau)}|(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)|\zeta\,dt\,dx\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{I},

where the last estimate holds if
a
is chosen sufficiently large. We do so now. The solid integral term
II_{9}
can be bounded as follows. Since
|\partial_{t}\zeta|\lesssim r^{-2}
we split the area of integration into two parts, the first one for
x_{n}/r\leq\varepsilon
and its complement.
On the set where
x_{n}/r\leq\varepsilon
we get that the integral is bounded by

(5.45)

\varepsilon\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\frac{1}{r}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx\leq\varepsilon\|\tilde{N}_{a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}

using (
5.42
).
The remaining part of the integral is bounded by
\frac{C}{r}\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt
.

Another term with a similar estimate is
V
; the fact that
\partial_{x_{n}}\zeta
vanishes on the set

(-\infty,r_{0}+r)\cup(r_{0}+2r,\infty)
means that it is supported away from the boundary of
\partial\Omega
and therefore,

(5.46)

|V|\leq\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)\cap\left\{x_{n}\geq r_{0}+r\right\}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx\leq\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx.

This leaves the term
II_{8}
where we introduce
x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\partial^{2}_{x_{n}x_{n}}(x_{n}^{2})
in order to integrate by parts.

(5.47)

\displaystyle 2II_{8}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial^{2}_{tx_{n}}(a_{nn}^{-1})(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\partial_{x_{n}}(u_{k}^{p/2})x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}x_{n}^{2}(\partial_{x_{n}}\zeta)\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dS=:II_{11}+II_{12}+II_{13}+II_{14}.

Here, using the Carleson condition for
|\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, term
II_{12}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle|II_{12}|\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|\partial_{t}a_{nn}||u_{k}^{p/2}-c||\nabla_{x}(u_{k}^{p/2})|\,x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}a_{nn}\right|^{2}\left|u_{k}^{p/2}-c\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\left|\partial_{t}a_{nn}\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}^{1/2}\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}\cdot\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}

(5.48)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx.

Since we assume
|\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\leq\delta
for sufficiently small
\delta>0
, term
II_{14}
is
bounded by
\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{I}
. For term
II_{13}
, by the same estimate and the fact that
|\nabla_{x}\zeta|\lesssim r^{-1}
, we have

|II_{13}|\lesssim\delta\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\frac{1}{r}\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

which therefore has the same bound as (
5.45
), again for small
\delta>0
.
This leaves the term
II_{11}
, where we integrate by parts in
t
. When
\partial_{t}
falls on
\zeta
we obtain a term similar to
II_{9}
since
|\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}
is bounded. The same is true for the resulting boundary term which has bounds identical to the term
II_{10}
. It remains to bound

\displaystyle\left|-\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{x_{n}}(a_{nn}^{-1})\partial_{t}[(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}]x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|\partial_{x_{n}}a_{nn}||u_{k}^{p/2}-c||\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})|\,x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{x_{n}}a_{nn}\right|\left|u_{k}^{p/2}-c\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\||\partial_{x_{n}}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}^{1/2}\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}\cdot\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

The constant in front of this term is small because the Carleson norm of the coefficient
a_{nn}
is small. The last term in this product can be further estimated as a product of the Area function and the nontangential maximal functions:

(5.49)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dx_{n}dx\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{2\Delta_{r}}{N}^{p-2}_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}u_{k}|^{2}y_{n}^{-n+2}dYds\right)dx\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{2\Delta_{r}}{N}^{p-2}_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}[|\nabla^{2}u_{k}|^{2}+y_{n}^{-2}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}]y_{n}^{-n+2}\right)dx\,dt,

since
|\partial_{t}u_{k}|=|\mathcal{L}u_{k}|\sim|\nabla_{x}^{2}u_{k}|+|\nabla A||\nabla u_{k}|
.
Caccioppoli inequality for the second gradient implies that for any
j\in\left\{1,\dots,n\right\}
, and for any parabolic cube
Q_{\rho}
with
Q_{2\rho}\subset\Omega
, there holds

\iint_{Q_{\rho}}|\nabla\partial_{j}u|^{2}dX\,dt\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\iint_{Q_{2\rho}}|\partial_{j}u|^{2}dX\,dt+\iint_{Q_{2\rho}}|\partial_{j}A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}dX\,dt.

For a parabolic Whitney cube
Q_{\rho}
, we further have
|\nabla A|\lesssim\rho^{-1}
in
Q_{2\rho}
, and so the above gives that
\iint_{Q_{\rho}}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dX\,dt\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\iint_{Q_{2\rho}}|\nabla u|^{2}dX\,dt
.
Since the set
\gamma_{a}(y,s)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)
can be decomposed as a union of Whitney cubes whose enlargements have finite overlap, we apply Caccioppoli inequality on each Whitney cube and the Hölder’s inequality with exponent
p/(p-2)
to get that

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt\\
\lesssim\|{N}_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}+\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}\,dX\,dt

Hence, after combining all estimates we have that

\displaystyle\left|-\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\partial_{x_{n}}(a_{nn}^{-1})\partial_{t}[(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}]x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dx_{n}\,dt\,dx\right|\lesssim\varepsilon\|\tilde{N}_{a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\|{N}_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}+\varepsilon\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(y,s)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}^{-n}\right)^{p/2}\,dX\,dt.

Finally, we put together all terms and integrate in
\theta
. Observing that
\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{12}\hbar)\subset\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)
, the above analysis ultimately yields (
5.29
).
Thus the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 5.50
.

Fix
p
such that
p/2
is a positive even integer. Let
\mathcal{L}
be an operator as in Lemma
5.28
and fix
a>0
sufficiently large as in Lemma
5.28
.
There exists
\kappa\in(0,1)
that depends only on
n
, constants
b>a
and
\gamma_{0}
that depend on
a
, and
C>0
that depends only on
n
and the ellipticity constants such that the following holds.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, if
\||\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}^{1/2}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}^{1/2}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is sufficiently small (depending on
\varepsilon
), where
\mathbf{a_{n}}
is the
n
-th row of the matrix
A
, then for any
u_{k}
as in Lemma
5.28
, for any
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0})
,
\beta>0
, there holds

(5.51)

\left|E_{1,\beta}\right|\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:M(\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2}))(x,t)>\kappa\beta\right\}\right|.

where

(5.52)

E_{1,\beta}:=\Big\{(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2})>\beta,\\
\varepsilon^{-1/p}S_{p,b}(u_{k})+{\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(u_{k})}\leq(\gamma\beta)^{2/p},\varepsilon^{1/p}\tilde{N}_{2,b}(u_{k})<(\gamma\beta)^{2/p}\Big\}.

Proof.

Let
b_{0}
and
\gamma_{0}
be as in Corollary
5.26
.
Let
\varepsilon>0
,
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0})
,
\beta>0
be fixed.
Observe that
E_{2,\beta}:=\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:M(\tilde{N}_{p,a}(u_{k}^{p/2}))(x,t)>\kappa\beta\right\}
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
. We can assume that
E_{2,\beta}
is a nonempty proper subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
, as otherwise the estimate (
5.51
) is trivial.
By Vitali’s covering lemma, we can find a non-overlapping collection of parabolic balls
\left\{\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\right\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

such that
2\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\subset E_{2,\beta}
,
10\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\cap E_{2,\beta}^{c}\neq\emptyset
for each
i
, and that
E_{2,\beta}\subset\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}5\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})
. Denote
\Delta_{i}:=5\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})
, and denote by
r_{i}
the radius of
\Delta_{i}
.
Set
F_{i}:=E_{1,\beta}\cap\Delta_{i}
. Note that
E_{1,\beta}\subset\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}F_{i}
as
E_{1,\beta}\subset E_{2,\beta}
.
The claim (
5.51
) will follow if we prove that
|F_{i}|\leq C\gamma^{2}|\Delta_{i}|
.
Let us denote

\eta_{L^{p}}(x,x_{n},t):=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{x_{n}/2}(x,x_{n},t)}\left|u_{k}(Y,s)\right|^{p}dYds\right)^{1/2},

where
Q_{x_{n}/2}(x,x_{n},t)
is a parabolic Whitney cube centered at
(x,x_{n},t)
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
. Take
b\geq b_{0}
and let
\hbar:=\hbar_{\beta,a}(\eta_{L^{p}})
be as in (
5.5
). By Corollary
5.26
,
(M_{\hbar}(\eta_{L^{p}}))\big(x,\hbar(x,t),t\big)\geq\,C\beta
for
(x,t)\in F_{i}
. We claim that this is also true when
M_{\hbar}(\eta_{L^{p}})
is replaced with the localized maximal function
M_{\hbar,6ar_{i}}(\eta_{L^{p}})
at scale
\sim r_{i}
. In fact, since
2\Delta_{i}\cap E_{2,\beta}^{c}\neq\emptyset
, we have

(5.53)

M(\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2}))(y,s)\leq\kappa\beta\quad\text{for some }(y,s)\in 2\Delta_{i}\cap E_{2,\beta}^{c}.

From this one deduces that

(5.54)

\eta_{L^{p}}(x,x_{n},t)\leq 2^{n+1}\kappa\beta\quad\text{for }(x,t)\in\Delta_{i}\text{ and }x_{n}>2r_{i}.

In fact, suppose that (
5.54
) is false for some
(Z,\tau)=(z,z_{n},\tau)
with
(z,\tau)\in\Delta_{i}
and
z_{n}>2r_{i}
. Then
\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2})(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})>2^{n+1}\kappa\beta
for all
(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\in S(Z,\tau)
, where
S(Z,\tau)
is defined as in (
5.3
), which is a parabolic ball centered at
(z,\tau)
of radius at least
2ar_{i}
. Therefore, since
a>1
,

\kappa\beta<\left(\frac{2a}{3+2a}\right)^{n+1}2^{n+1}\kappa\beta<\fint_{\Delta_{(3+2a)r_{i}}(y,s)}\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2})\,dx^{\prime}dt^{\prime}\leq M(\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2}))(y,s),

a contradiction to (
5.53
).

We choose
\kappa\leq 2^{-n-1}
. In view of (
5.54
), for
(x,t)\in F_{i}
,
\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2})(x,t)=\tilde{N}_{2,a}^{2r_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2})(x,t)
. It also guarantees that
\hbar\leq 2r_{i}
on
\Delta_{i}
. Moreover, it implies that the “radius” of the boundary ball
R
constructed in Lemma
5.17
is bounded by
6ar_{i}
. Therefore, we have justified that

(5.55)

\text{ for }(x,t)\in F_{i},\ (M_{\hbar,6ar_{i}}(\eta_{L^{p}}))\big(x,\hbar(x,t),t\big)\geq\,C\beta.

We define

(5.56)

\hbar_{i}(x,t):=\sup\left\{\hbar(x,t),\frac{1}{2}\dist_{p}\left((x,t),F_{i}\right)\right\},

and
\mathcal{S}_{i}:=\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{20r_{i}},\hbar_{i})
, where
\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{20r_{i}},\hbar_{i})
is defined as in Lemma
5.28
. One can easily check that
\hbar_{i}
satisfies (
5.14
), (
5.15
), and (
5.16
), and enjoys in addition the property

(5.57)

\text{for any }(x,t)\in 40a\,\Delta_{i}\setminus F_{i},\ \exists\,(y,s)\in F_{i}\text{ such that }\quad\mathcal{S}_{i}\cap\gamma_{2a}(x,t)\subset\gamma_{b}(y,s)

for
b\geq b_{0}
sufficiently large.
Property (
5.57
) holds because we ensure that the graph of
\hbar_{i}
is sufficiently far away from the boundary even when it is not above
F_{i}
.
We choose the constant

c:=\fint_{t\in I_{i}^{0}}\int_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}u_{k}^{p/2}(X,t)\eta^{2}(X)\,dX\,dt\geq 0.

Here
\eta
is a smooth nonnegative cutoff
function in spatial variables, supported in
B_{i}^{0}
and satisfying
\int_{B_{i}^{0}}\eta^{2}\,dX=1
,
|\nabla\eta|\lesssim r_{i}^{-1}\eta
, and

\sup_{B_{i}^{0}}\eta^{2}\leq\fint_{B_{i}^{0}}\eta^{2}\,dX
.
I_{i}^{0}
is an interval in
\mathbb{R}
centered at
t_{i}
with length
\sim r_{i}^{2}
and
B_{i}^{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a ball with radius
\sim r_{i}
located so that
B_{i}^{0}\times I_{i}^{0}\subset\mathcal{S}_{i}\cap\left\{(x,x_{n},t):(x,t)\in\Delta_{i},x_{n}>2r_{i}\right\}
.
By (
5.54
),
0\leq c\leq|\eta_{L^{p}}|\leq 2^{n+1}\kappa\beta
. Then by (
5.55
), we have

C^{2}\beta^{2}\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\int_{F_{i}}(M_{\hbar,6ar_{i}}(\eta_{L^{p}}))\big(x,\hbar(x,t),t\big)^{2}dx\,dt\\
\leq\int_{F_{i}}(M_{\hbar,6ar_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)_{L^{2}})\big(x,\hbar(x,t),t\big)^{2}dx\,dt+2^{2n+2}\kappa^{2}\beta^{2}\left|F_{i}\right|.

We take
\kappa\leq 2^{-n-1}
small enough so that
2^{2n+2}\kappa^{2}<C^{2}/2
, and so we can hide the last term to the left-hand side. Then

\frac{C^{2}}{2}\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\int_{F_{i}}(M_{\hbar,6ar_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)_{L^{2}})\big(x,\hbar(x,t),t\big)^{2}dx\,dt\\
\leq\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\int_{\Delta_{i}}(M_{\hbar_{i},6ar_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)_{L^{2}})\big(x,\hbar_{i}(x,t),t\big)^{2}dx\,dt.

Notice that we changed
\hbar
to
\hbar_{i}
in the last inequality; this is valid because
\hbar_{i}=\hbar
on
F_{i}
. By the
L^{2}
boundedness of the localized maximal function, we obtain that

(5.58)

C^{\prime}\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\int_{10\Delta_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)_{L^{2}}(x,\hbar_{i}(x,t),t)^{2}dx\,dt.

We claim that

(5.59)

\int_{10\Delta_{i}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)^{2}_{L^{2}}(x,\hbar_{i}(x,t),t)dx\,dt\leq C\int_{1/6}^{6}\int_{20\Delta_{i}}\left(u_{k}^{p/2}-c\right)^{2}(x,\theta\hbar_{i}(x,t),t)dx\,dtd\theta.

This has been established in
[
14
]
and this claim is a mild modification of the elliptic argument in
[
12
, Lemma 5.6]
.
Hence it follows that by Lemma
5.28
we get that

(5.60)

C^{\prime}\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\frac{C}{\beta^{2}}(1+\varepsilon^{-1})\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\\
+\frac{\varepsilon}{\beta^{2}}\left(\|\tilde{N}_{2,a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\right)\|_{L^{2}(40\Delta_{i})}^{2}+\|N_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\|_{L^{p}(40a\Delta_{i})}^{p}\right)\\
+\frac{\varepsilon}{\beta^{2}}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\right)^{p/2}+\frac{C}{\beta^{2}r_{i}}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt,

where
\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{S}_{i}\cap\left\{x_{n}>\varepsilon\,r\right\}
.
Our goal is to bound the right-hand side of (
5.60
) by
C\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}\left|\Delta_{i}\right|
, which will imply the desired estimate (
5.51
) by summing over
i
.
To this end, we write

\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\leq C\int_{40\Delta_{i}}\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}(y,y_{n},s)\frac{dydy_{n}ds}{y_{n}^{n}}dx\,dt.

When
(x,t)\in F_{i}
, it holds trivially that
\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}(y,y_{n},s)\frac{dydy_{n}ds}{y_{n}^{n}}\leq S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x,t)^{p}
.
When
(x,t)\in 40\Delta_{i}\setminus F_{i}
, by (
5.57
), we can find some
(x_{0},t_{0})\in F_{i}
so that

\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}(y,y_{n},s)\frac{dydy_{n}ds}{y_{n}^{n}}\leq S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x_{0},t_{0})^{p}
. Hence, by the definition of
E_{1,\beta}
, we conclude that

(5.61)

\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}(y,y_{n},s)\frac{dydy_{n}ds}{y_{n}^{n}}\leq\varepsilon\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}\quad\text{for all }(x,t)\in 40\Delta_{i},

and so

(5.62)

\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\leq C\varepsilon\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}\left|\Delta_{i}\right|.

Similar consideration holds for the term

\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}(y,y_{n},s)\frac{dydy_{n}ds}{y_{n}^{n}}\right)^{p/2}

which is
\leq S_{2,b}(u_{k})(x,t)^{p}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}
when
(x,t)\in F_{i}
, with analogous estimate outside
F_{i}
.

The terms
\|\tilde{N}_{2,a}((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\|_{L^{2}(40\Delta_{i})}^{2}
and
\|N_{a}(u_{k}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\|_{L^{p}(40\Delta_{i})}^{p}
are controlled similarly. Recall that since
\left|c\right|\leq|\eta_{L^{p}}|
has the bound given in (
5.54
) the contribution of this term is at most
C\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}|\Delta_{i}|
.
We note that for any
(x,t)\in 40\Delta_{i}
, there exists
(x_{0},t_{0})\in F_{i}
such that
N_{a}(u_{k}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})^{p/2}(x,t)\lesssim\tilde{N}_{2,2a}(u_{k}^{p/2}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})(x,t)\leq\tilde{N}_{2,b}(u_{k})(x_{0},t_{0})^{p/2}
due to (
5.57
) and the fact that
L^{\infty}
version of the nontangential maximal function for solutions
u_{k}
is controlled by
L^{p}
version of this function for cones of slightly enlarged aperture. Therefore, by the definition of
F_{i}
we get that

(5.63)

\varepsilon\|\tilde{N}_{a}(u_{k}^{p/2}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\|_{L^{2}(40\Delta_{i})}^{2}+\varepsilon\|N_{a}(u_{k}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}_{i}})\|_{L^{p}(40\Delta_{i})}^{p}\leq C\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}\left|\Delta_{i}\right|.

For the term
\frac{1}{r_{i}}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt
, we first use the triangle inequality to write

\left|u_{k}^{p/2}(X,t)-c\right|^{2}\leq 2\left|u_{k}^{p/2}(X,t)-\fint_{B_{i}^{0}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)dY\right|^{2}\\
+2\left|\fint_{B_{i}^{0}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)dY-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)\eta^{2}(Y)dY\right|+2\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)\eta^{2}(Y)dY-c\right|^{2},

and we control the term
\frac{1}{r_{i}}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt
by the sum of 3 integrals corresponding to the 3 terms on the right-hand side. We call the 3 integrals
J_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
. For
J_{1}
, we use the Poincaré inequality in the spatial variables to get that

J_{1}\leq Cr_{i}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}\left|\nabla(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}dX\,dt\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx,

where in the last step we recalled that
x_{n}>\varepsilon r_{i}
in
\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}
. So this term is again bounded by a bound similar to (
5.62
).
To estimate
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
, we denote by
c(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)\eta^{2}(Y)dY
, and denote by
I_{i}
the time interval of
\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}
. We write

J_{2}\leq Cr_{i}^{n-1}\int_{I_{i}}\left|\fint_{B_{i}^{0}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)dY-c(t)\right|^{2}dt\\
=Cr_{i}^{n-1}\int_{I_{i}}\left|\int_{Z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(u^{p/2}(Z,t)-\fint_{B_{i}^{0}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)dY\right)\eta^{2}(Z)dZ\right|^{2}dt\\
\leq\frac{C}{r_{i}}\int_{I_{i}}\int_{B_{i}^{0}}\left|u^{p/2}(Z,t)-\fint_{B_{i}^{0}}u_{k}^{p/2}(Y,t)dY\right|^{2}dZ\,dt.

By the Poincaré inequality in the spatial variables again and the observation that
I_{i}\times B_{i}^{0}\subset\mathcal{S}_{i}\cap\left\{x_{n}>2r_{i}\right\}
,

J_{2}
can be bounded as
J_{1}
. For
J_{3}
, we observe that for
t\in I_{i}
,

|c(t)-c|\leq\sup_{t,t^{\prime}\in I_{i}}|c(t)-c(t^{\prime})|\leq\sup_{t,t^{\prime}\in I_{i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\left|u_{k}^{p}(X,t)-u_{k}^{p}(X,t^{\prime})\right|\right)^{1/2}\eta^{2}(X)dX\\
\leq\sup_{t,t^{\prime}\in I_{i}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|u_{k}^{p}(X,t)-u_{k}^{p}(X,t^{\prime})\right|\eta^{2}(X)dX\right)^{1/2}.

Then we can use the PDE of
\partial_{t}u_{k}
as in (
5.22
) to estimate
|c(t)-c|^{2}
and get that for
t\in I_{i}

(5.64)

|c(t)-c|^{2}\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times I_{i}}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(r_{i}^{-2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times I_{i}}|u_{k}|^{p}\eta^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times I_{i}}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\\
\leq C(1+\varepsilon^{-1})r_{i}^{-n}\iint_{B_{i}^{0}\times I_{i}}|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}dX\,dt+\varepsilon\,\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{B_{i}^{0}\times I_{i}}|u_{k}|^{p}dX\,dt.

Since
B_{i}^{0}\times I_{i}\subset\mathcal{S}_{i}\cap\left\{x_{n}>2r_{i}\right\}
, for any
(X,t)\in B_{i}^{0}\times I_{i}
, there exists some
(x_{0},t_{0})\in F_{i}
such that
|u_{k}|^{p}(X,t)\leq N_{b}(u_{k})^{p}(x_{0},t_{0})
. Therefore,

J_{3}\leq Cr_{i}^{n+1}\sup_{t\in I_{i}}|c(t)-c|^{2}\leq C\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}r_{i}^{n+1}
, and after adding up all estimates we obtain the desired bound:
|F_{i}|\leq C\gamma^{2}|\Delta_{i}|
.

∎

We can now establish Theorem
5.6
. Denote by
\eta=u_{k}^{p/2}
and consider
1<q<\infty
.
We assume a priori that
\|\tilde{N}(\eta)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
. Consider any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. By Lemma
5.50
, there exists
\delta(\varepsilon)>0
such that the conclusion of Lemma
5.50
holds whenever
\||\nabla\mathbf{a_{n}}|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}^{1/2}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}^{1/2}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta(\varepsilon)
. Then

\left\|\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}^{q}=q\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{q-1}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)(x,t)>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu\\
\leq q\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{q-1}\left|E_{1,\nu}\right|d\nu+q\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{q-1}\left|\left\{\varepsilon^{-1/p}S_{p,b}(u_{k})+\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(u_{k})\right\}>(\gamma\nu)^{2/p}\right|d\nu\\
+q\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{q-1}\left|\left\{\varepsilon^{1/p}{\tilde{N}_{b}(u_{k})}>(\gamma\nu)^{2/p}\right\}\right|d\nu,

where
E_{1,\nu}
is defined as in (
5.52
).
By Lemma
5.50
and change of variables, one has

\left\|\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}^{q}\leq C\gamma^{2}q\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{q-1}\left|\left\{M(\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta))>\kappa\nu\right\}\right|d\nu\\
+(\gamma\varepsilon^{1/2}/2)^{-q}pq/2\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{pq/2-1}\left|\left\{S_{p,b}(u_{k})>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu\\
+(\gamma\varepsilon^{-1/2}/2)^{-q}pq/2\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{pq/2-1}\left|\left\{S_{2,b}(u_{k})>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu+\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{pq/2-1}\left|\left\{\tilde{N}_{b}(u_{k})>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu\right]\\
=\kappa^{-q}\gamma^{2}q\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{q-1}\left|\left\{M(\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta))>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu+C(\gamma,\varepsilon)\left\|S_{p,b}(u_{k})\right\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}\\
+(\gamma\varepsilon^{-1/2}/2)^{-q}\left[\|{S_{2,b}}(u_{k})\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}+\|\tilde{N}_{b}(u_{k})\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}\right]\\
=\kappa^{-q}\gamma^{2}\|M(\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta))\|_{L^{q}}^{q}+C(\gamma,\varepsilon)\left\|S_{p,b}(u_{k})\right\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}+(\gamma\varepsilon^{-1/2}/2)^{-q}\left[\|{S_{2,b}}(u_{k})\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}+\|\tilde{N}_{b}(u_{k})\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}\right].

As the maximal function
M
is
L^{q}
bounded for
q>1
the first term of the last line is further bounded by

C(q)\kappa^{-q}\gamma^{2}\|\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)\|_{L^{q}}^{q}
.
Choose
\gamma\in(0,1)
sufficiently small so that
C(q)\kappa^{-q}\gamma^{2}<1/4
, as well as
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
so small such that

(\gamma/2)^{-q}\varepsilon^{q/2}\left[\|{S_{2,b}}(u_{k})\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}+\|\tilde{N}_{b}(u_{k})\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}\right]<1/4\|\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)\|_{L^{q}}^{q},

which is possible since the inequality
S_{2}\lesssim N
holds for solutions
u_{k}
in
L^{pq/2}
norm.
Hence, we can hide
\|\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)\|_{L^{q}}^{q}
on the left-hand side and obtain the estimate:

\|\tilde{N}_{a}(\eta)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}^{q}\leq 2C(\gamma,\varepsilon)\left\|S_{p,b}(u_{k})\right\|_{L^{pq/2}}^{pq/2}.

As
\gamma,\,\varepsilon
are fixed now we drop the dependence of the constant on them.
Recall that
\eta=u_{k}^{p/2}
. It follows we may restate the inequality above as follows. For all
p/2<r<\infty

it holds that

\|\tilde{N}_{a}(u_{k})\|_{L^{r}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C_{r}\left\|S_{p,b}(u_{k})\right\|_{L^{r}}.

This proves Theorem
5.6
, as the claim for
u
follows by taking the limit
k\to\infty

in the inequality above.∎

5.2.
N<S_{p}
for
\nabla_{T}u

Theorem 5.65
.

Let
A
be a matrix with bounded measurable coefficients that satisfies the ellipticity condition, and that

\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{\mathcal{C}}<\infty
.
Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
.
For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta>0
such that if

\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\left\||\nabla_{T}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

then for any
p
such that
p/2
is a positive even integer, for any
a>0
,

f\in L^{q}(\partial\Omega)\cap\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{-1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega)
,
there exists a
constant
C=C(n,p,a,\varepsilon,\lambda,\Lambda)>0
, such that for the energy solution
1
1

1

As can be seen from the proof, the boundary condition does not matter. This remark applies also to Theorem
5.84
, Lemmas
6.1
and
6.24
.

u
to
\mathcal{L}u=0
,

\partial^{A}_{\nu}u\Big|_{\partial\Omega}=f
,
there holds for all
q>p/2

(5.66)

\|{\tilde{N}}(\nabla_{T}u)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C\left\|S_{p}(\nabla_{T}u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\tilde{N}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|S_{2}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\right),

provided we know a priori that
\|\tilde{N}(\nabla_{T}u)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
.

We shall only highlight the modifications needed from Subsection
5.1
. First, we change the definition of
w
: it is now the
L^{p}
average of
|\nabla_{T}u|
, that is, for
(X,t)=(x,x_{n},t)\in\Omega
,

(5.67)

w(X,t)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{x_{n}/2}(X,t)}|\nabla_{T}u(Y,s)|^{p}\,dY\,ds\right)^{1/p}=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{x_{n}/2}(X,t)}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}|\partial_{y_{i}}u(Y,s)|^{2}\right)^{p/2}\,dY\,ds\right)^{1/p}.

We also introduce for
k=1,\dots,n-1
,

(5.68)

w_{k}(X,t):=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{x_{n}/2}(X,t)}|\partial_{y_{k}}u(Y,s)|^{p}\,dY\,ds\right)^{1/p},\quad\text{and }u_{k}:=\partial_{k}u.

Lemma 5.69
.

Let
k\in\left\{1,2,\dots,n-1\right\}
. Let
w_{k}
be defined as in (
5.68
) and let
\hbar_{\nu,a}
be defined as in (
5.5
).
Then for any
a>0
there exists
b_{0}=b_{0}(a)>a
and
\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{0}(a)>0
such that the following holds.
Having fixed an arbitrary
\nu>0
,
b\geq b_{0}
, and
\gamma\leq\gamma_{0}
for each point
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
from the set

(5.70)

\big\{(x,t):\,N_{a}(w_{k})(x,t)>\nu\mbox{ and }S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x,t)+\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}N_{b}(\nabla u)\leq\gamma\nu\big\}

there exists a boundary ball
R
with
(x,t)\in K_{0}R
for some
K_{0}=K_{0}(n,p,a)>1
and such that

(5.71)

\big|w_{k}\big(z,\hbar_{\nu,a}(w_{k})(z,\tau),\tau\big)\big|>\nu/{8}\,\,\text{ for all }\,\,(z,\tau)\in R.

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
5.17
. The first modification is needed in (
5.22
), where the PDE of
u_{k}
is used. Since
u_{k}
satisfies

(5.72)

-\partial_{t}u_{k}+\divg(A\nabla u_{k})=-\divg((\partial_{x_{k}}A)\nabla u),

there is one more term in (
5.22
). That is, we have

(5.73)

v_{av}(t_{0}^{\prime})-v_{av}(t_{0})=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\{t_{0}^{\prime}\}}|u_{k}|^{p}\eta^{2}\,dX-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\{t_{0}\}}|u_{k}|^{p}\eta^{2}\,dX\\
=-2p\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla\eta)|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\eta\,dX\,dt\\
-p(p-1)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k})|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt+p\,I,

where

I:=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}\divg((\partial_{k}A)\nabla u)|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\eta^{2}dX\,dt

is the new term coming from the right-hand side of (
5.72
). Integrating by parts, we get

I=-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(\partial_{k}A)\nabla u\cdot\nabla\left(|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\eta^{2}\right)dX\,dt\\
=-(p-1)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(\partial_{k}A)\nabla u\cdot\nabla u_{k}|u_{k}|^{p-3}u_{k}\eta^{2}dX\,dt\\
-2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(\partial_{k}A)\nabla u\cdot\nabla\eta|u_{k}|^{p-2}u_{k}\eta dX\,dt=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

By Cauchy-Schwarz,

|I_{1}|\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}|\partial_{k}A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}\eta^{2}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\lesssim\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}S_{p,b}(u_{k})^{p/2}(x,t)N_{b}(\nabla u)^{p/2}(x,t)\\
\leq CS_{p,b}(u_{k})^{p}(x,t)+\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}N_{b}(\nabla u)^{p}(x,t)

using the properties of
\eta
and the fact that
I_{y_{n}}(s)\times\widetilde{B}\subset Q_{4y_{n}/5}\subset\gamma_{b}(x,t)
. For
I_{2}
, we use the bound of
|\nabla\eta|
and get that

|I_{2}|\lesssim\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}|\partial_{k}A||\nabla u|^{p}\eta^{2}y_{n}^{-1}dX\,dt\lesssim\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}N_{b}(\nabla u)^{p}(x,t).

Altogether, we have that
for any
t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}\in I_{y_{n}}(s)
,

\left|v_{av}(t_{0}^{\prime})-v_{av}(t_{0})\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)+CS_{p,b}(u_{k})^{p}(x,t)+C\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}N_{b}(\nabla u)^{p}(x,t).

The condition
S_{p,b}(u_{k})(x,t)+\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}N_{b}(\nabla u)(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu
ensures that the rest of the argument goes exactly as in the proof of Lemma
5.17
.
∎

Corollary 5.74
.

Let
w
be defined as in (
5.67
),
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
and fix
a>0
.
Associated with these, let
b_{0},\gamma_{0}
be as in Lemma
5.69
. Then there exists a finite
constant
C=C(n,p,a)>0
with the property that for any
\nu>0
,
b\geq b_{0}
,
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0}]
, and any point
(x,t)\in\left\{(x,t):N_{a}(w)(x,t)>\nu\right\}

such that
S_{p,b}(\nabla_{T}u)(x,t)+\left\||\nabla_{T}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}N_{b}(\nabla u)(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu
, one has

(M_{\hbar_{\nu,a}}w)\big(x,\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t),t\big)\geq\,C\nu,

where
M_{\hbar_{\nu,a}}
is the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function on the graph of
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)
.

Proof.

Note that for any
(x,t)
at which
N_{a}(w)(x,t)>\nu
, there exists
k\in\left\{1,\dots,n-1\right\}
such that
N_{a}(w_{k})>\nu/(n-1)
. Then the corollary follows from Lemma
5.69
.
∎

The following lemma is the analog of Lemma
5.28
.

Lemma 5.75
.

Fix
p
such that
p/2
is an even positive integer.
Let
\Omega={\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
and let
{\mathcal{L}}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
be a parabolic operator.
Let
u
be an energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
and let
u_{k}=\partial_{x_{k}}u
with
k\in\left\{1,\dots,n-1\right\}
. For a fixed (sufficiently large)
a>0
determined below,, consider an arbitrary function
\hbar:{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that it satisfies
the estimates (
5.14
), (
5.15
) and
\hbar\geq 0
.
Then
we have the following:

For any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0

such that if

\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}<\infty
and

\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

then
for all arbitrary parabolic surface balls
\Delta_{r}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
of radius
r
such that at least one point of
\Delta_{r}

satisfies
\hbar(x,t)\leq 2r
, we have the following estimate for an arbitrary
c\geq 0
:

(5.76)

\int_{1/6}^{6}\int_{\Delta_{r}}\big|u^{p/2}_{k}\big(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t\big)-c\big|^{2}\,dx\,dt\,d\theta\leq C(1+\varepsilon^{-1})\iint_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\\
+\varepsilon\left(\|\tilde{N}_{2,a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\right)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{N}_{a}(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\right)\\
+\varepsilon\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}dX\,dt+\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt,

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
that only depends on
a,\Lambda,n
but not on
k
,
u_{k}
,
c
,
\varepsilon
or
\Delta_{r}
.
Here,

\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar):=\left\{(x,x_{n},t):\,(x,t)\in\Delta_{2r}\text{ and }\frac{\hbar(x,t)}{12}<x_{n}<18r\right\},

where

\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)\cap\left\{(x,x_{n},t):x_{n}>\varepsilon r\right\}
.
The cones used to define the nontangential
maximal functions in this lemma have vertices on
\partial\Omega
.

Proof.

The first modification in the proof of Lemma
5.28
happens when dealing with the term
II
from (
5.1
), where the PDE of
u_{k}^{p/2}-c
is used. In the current setting, there is an extra term from
\mathcal{L}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)
, namely, we have

{\mathcal{L}}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)=\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)u_{k}^{p/2-2}A\nabla u_{k}\cdot\nabla u_{k}+\frac{p}{2}u_{k}^{p/2-1}\divg((\partial_{k}A)\nabla u).

As a result, we get an extra term
J
in (
5.1
) and we write

(5.77)

II=\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{C}+\mathcal{D}+II_{7}+J,

where

J:=p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)u_{k}^{p/2-1}\divg((\partial_{k}A)\nabla u)x_{n}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

and the terms
\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
, and
II_{7}
are the same as in (
5.1
). By the product rule,
J
is further split into two terms:
J=J_{1}+J_{2}
, where

J_{1}:=p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)u_{k}^{p/2-1}(\partial_{k}A)\Delta u\,x_{n}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

J_{2}:=p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)u_{k}^{p/2-1}\nabla(\partial_{k}A)\nabla u\,x_{n}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx.

By Cauchy-Schwarz and the Carleson condition on
\partial_{k}A
, we get that

(5.78)

|J_{1}|\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}\left(u_{k}^{p/2}-c\right)^{2}\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\\
\lesssim\left(\int_{2\Delta_{r}}N_{a}^{p-2}(u_{k}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})(x,t)\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla^{2}u(Y,s)|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\,dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\left\||\partial_{k}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}^{1/2}\left(\int_{\Delta_{2r}}N_{a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)^{2}dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

Applying Hölder’s inequality (with exponent
p/(p-2)
) to the first term and then using Young’s inequality, one gets that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

|J_{1}|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\partial_{k}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N_{a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(2\Delta_{r})}^{2}\\
+\varepsilon\left\|N_{a}(u_{k}{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(2\Delta_{r})}^{p}+\varepsilon\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}\,dX\,dt.

For
J_{2}
, we integrate by parts again by pulling off
\partial_{k}A
. Since
k<n
, it gives 5 terms as follows.
J_{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{4}J_{2j}+J_{2b}
, where

J_{21}=-p(p/2-1)\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(\partial_{k}u_{k})u_{k}^{p/2-2}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\nabla A\cdot\nabla u\,x_{n}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

J_{22}=-p^{2}/2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(\partial_{k}u_{k})u_{k}^{p-2}\nabla A\cdot\nabla u\,x_{n}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

J_{23}=-p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}u_{k}^{p/2-1}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\nabla A\cdot\nabla\partial_{k}u\,x_{n}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

J_{24}=-p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}u_{k}^{p/2-1}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\nabla A\cdot\nabla u\,x_{n}\,\partial_{k}\zeta dx_{n}\,dt\,dx,

and the boundary term

(5.79)

J_{2b}=p\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)u_{k}^{p/2-1}\nabla A\cdot\nabla u\,x_{n}\zeta\nu_{k}\,dS.

The terms
J_{21}
and
J_{23}
are similar as
J_{1}
and satisfy

|J_{21}|+|J_{23}|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N_{a}\left((u_{k}^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(2\Delta_{r})}^{2}\\
+\varepsilon\left\|N_{a}(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(2\Delta_{r})}^{p}+\varepsilon\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla u_{k}\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}\,dX\,dt.

For the term
J_{22}
, we have

|J_{22}|\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{k}u_{k}\right|^{2}u_{k}^{p-2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{p}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{k}u_{k}|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N_{a}(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}.

For
J_{24}
, we use Cauchy-Schwarz, the bound
|\partial_{k}\zeta|\leq C/r
, and the estimate (
5.42
) to obtain that

|J_{24}|\leq C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}^{1/2}\left\|N_{a}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p/2}\\
\leq\varepsilon\left\|N_{a}(u_{k}^{p/2}-c)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}.

We are left with the boundary term
J_{2b}
. By the computation of
\nu_{k}
on the graph of
\theta\hbar
(see (
5.38
)) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, one has

|J_{2b}|\leq\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left|u_{k}^{p/2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)-c\right|^{2}\zeta\,dx\,dt\\
+C\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)\hbar(x,t)^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t)\zeta\,dx\,dt=\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{I}+J_{2b^{\prime}}.

The term
\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{I}
can be absorbed into the left-hand side of (
5.76
). For the term
J_{2b^{\prime}}
, we integrate in
\theta
, noting the change of variable
dx_{n}=\hbar(x,t)d\theta
, and get that

\int_{1/6}^{6}J_{2b^{\prime}}\,d\theta\leq C\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
\leq C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}.

The last modification is required for estimating
\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt
as in (
5.49
), where the PDE for
u_{k}
is used. We start with Fubini’s theorem as before, and write

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}u_{k}\right|^{2}|u_{k}|^{p-2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt\\
\lesssim\int_{2\Delta_{r}}{N}^{p-2}_{a}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a/2}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}u_{k}|^{2}y_{n}^{-n+2}dYds\right)dx\,dt.

Following the notation (
2.18
), we write for any set
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(5.80)

A_{a/2}\left(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{E}\right)=\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a/2}(x,t)\cap E}|\partial_{t}u_{k}|^{2}y_{n}^{-n+2}dYds\right)^{1/2}.

Using the arguments in
[
14
, Section 6]
(or (
2.20
