)), we can get that for any
q>1
, there holds

(5.81)

\left\|A_{a/2}(u_{k}\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{q}(\Delta_{2r})}\lesssim\left(\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{q/2}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/q}\\
+\left\|\mu\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}\left\|N_{a}(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{q}}.

Then using (
5.81
) with
q=p
, and Young’s inequality with exponent
p/(p-2)
, one sees that

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt\leq C\left\|N_{a}(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\\
+C\int_{\Delta_{20ar}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}\,dX\,dt.

Here, we don’t need the constants to be small as the term
\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt
is already multiplied by
\varepsilon
.
We are done with the modifications.
∎

Lemma 5.82
.

Fix
p
such that
p/2
is a positive even integer. Let
k\in\left\{1,\dots,n-1\right\}
, and let
\mathcal{L}
be an operator as in Lemma
5.75
that satisfies
\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{\mathcal{C}}<\infty
. Fix
a>0
sufficiently large as in Lemma
5.75
.
There exists
\kappa\in(0,1)
that depends only on
n
, constants
b>a
and
\gamma_{0}
that depend on
a
, and
C>0
that depends only on
n
and the ellipticity constants such that the following holds.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, if
\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\left\||\partial_{k}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is sufficiently small (depending on
\varepsilon
), then for any
u_{k}
as in Lemma
5.75
, for any
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0})
,
\beta>0
, there holds

\left|E_{1,\beta}\right|\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:M(\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2}))(x,t)>\kappa\beta\right\}\right|.

where

E_{1,\beta}:=\Big\{(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,\tilde{N}_{2,a}(u_{k}^{p/2})>\beta,\\
\varepsilon^{-1/p}S_{p,b}(u_{k})+{\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(\nabla u)}\leq(\gamma\beta)^{2/p},\varepsilon^{1/p}\tilde{N}_{2,b}(\nabla u)<(\gamma\beta)^{2/p}\Big\}.

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
5.50
with little change. The only place where the PDE is used is in (
5.64
) when controlling
\frac{1}{r_{i}}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|u_{k}^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt
from (
5.60
). In the current setting, we pick up a new term as explained in (
5.73
). Modifications are straightforward using ideas below (
5.23
); we skip the details.
∎

From Lemma
5.82
, Theorem
5.65
follows by a standard argument similar to that at the end of Section
5.1
. In fact, we can get a more precise estimate than (
5.66
), namely, we get that for
k\in\left\{1,\dots,n-1\right\}
,

(5.83)

\|{\tilde{N}}(\partial_{k}u)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left\|S_{p}(\partial_{k}u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\tilde{N}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|S_{2}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\right),

provided we know a priori that
\|\tilde{N}(\partial_{k}u)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
.

5.3.
N<S_{p}
for
H

In this subsection we derive the following theorem for
H=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{nj}\partial_{j}u
.

Theorem 5.84
.

Let
A
be a matrix with bounded measurable coefficients that satisfies the ellipticity condition.
Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
.
For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta>0
such that if

\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\||\nabla^{2}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}A|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

then for any
p
such that
p/2
is a positive even integer, for any
a>0
,

f\in L^{q}(\partial\Omega)\cap\dot{H}{\vphantom{H}}^{-1/4}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}(\partial\Omega)
,
there exists a
constant
C=C(n,p,a,\varepsilon,\lambda,\Lambda)>0
, such that for the energy solution
u
to
\mathcal{L}u=0
,

\partial^{A}_{\nu}u\Big|_{\partial\Omega}=f
,
there holds for all
q>p/2

(5.85)

\|{\tilde{N}}(H)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C\left\|S_{p}(H)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\tilde{N}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|S_{2}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}\right),

provided we know a priori that
\|\tilde{N}(H)\|_{L^{q}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
.

As before, we only highlight the modifications needed from Subsections
5.1
and
5.2
.
In this subsection,
w
is the
L^{p}
average of
H
, that is, for
(X,t)=(x,x_{n},t)\in\Omega
,

(5.86)

w(X,t)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{Q_{x_{n}/2}(X,t)}|H|^{p}\,dY\,ds\right)^{1/p}.

Lemma 5.87
.

For any
a>0
there exists
b_{0}=b_{0}(a)>a
and
\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{0}(a)>0
such that the following holds.
Having fixed an arbitrary
\nu>0
,
b\geq b_{0}
,
\gamma\leq\gamma_{0}
, and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, for each point
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
from the set

(5.88)

\big\{(x,t):\,N_{a}(H)(x,t)>\nu,\,S_{p,b}(H)+\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(\nabla u)(x,t)+\delta(\varepsilon,A)N_{b}(\nabla u)\leq\gamma\nu\big\},

where
\delta(\varepsilon,A):=\varepsilon^{-1/(p(p-1))}\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/(p-1)}+\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2/p}+\left\||\partial_{t}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}+\left\||\nabla^{2}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}
,
there exists a boundary ball
R
with
(x,t)\in K_{0}R
with
K_{0}=K_{0}(n,p,a)>1
and such that

(5.89)

\big|w\big(z,\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(z,\tau),\tau\big)\big|>\nu/{8}\,\,\text{ for all }\,\,(z,\tau)\in R.

Proof.

We adopt the notations in Lemma
5.17
(and Lemma
5.69
), keeping in mind that
u_{k}
is replaced by
H
. We use the PDE (
4.2
) for
H
, and so we need to modify our estimates on the function
v_{av}(\tau):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|H|^{p}(X,\tau)\eta^{2}(X)\,dX
.
We have

v_{av}(t_{0}^{\prime})-v_{av}(t_{0})=-2p\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A^{T}\nabla H\cdot\nabla\eta)|H|^{p-2}H\eta\,dX\,dt\\
-p(p-1)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}(A^{T}\nabla H\cdot\nabla H)|H|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\,dX\,dt+p\,I,

where
I:=-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}F|H|^{p-2}H\eta^{2}dX\,dt
and
F
is as defined in (
4.2
). We use the bound (
4.3
) on
F
and get that

|I|\lesssim\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}\left(|\nabla A||\partial_{t}u|+|\partial_{t}A||\nabla u|+|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|+|\nabla A||\nabla^{2}u|+|\nabla^{2}A||\nabla u|\right)|H|^{p-1}\eta^{2}.

Since
|\partial_{t}u|\sim|\nabla^{2}u|+\left|\nabla A\right|\left|\nabla u\right|
, we can simplify the above as

|I|\lesssim\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}|\nabla A||\nabla^{2}u||H|^{p-1}\eta^{2}+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}\left(|\nabla A|^{2}+|\partial_{t}A|+|\nabla^{2}A|\right)|\nabla u||H|^{p-1}\eta^{2}\\
=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

By Cauchy-Schwarz, the property of
\eta
, and the fact that
Q_{4y_{n}/5}\subset\gamma_{b}(x,t)
,

I_{1}\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}|H|^{p-2}\eta^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]}|\nabla A|^{2}|H|^{p}\eta^{2}\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}S_{2}(\nabla u)(x,t)N(H)^{p-1}(x,t)\\
\leq\varepsilon S_{2}(\nabla u)^{p}(x,t)+C\varepsilon^{-1/(p-1)}\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{p/(p-1)}N_{b}(\nabla u)^{p}(x,t),

using the observation that
|H|\lesssim|\nabla u|
in the last inequality.
For
I_{2}
, we use again that
|H|\lesssim|\nabla u|
to get

I_{2}\leq C\left(\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\||\partial_{t}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\||\nabla^{2}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)N_{b}(\nabla u)^{p}(x,t).

Since
\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(\nabla u)(x,t)+\delta(\varepsilon,A)N_{b}(\nabla u)\leq\gamma\nu
, we can proceed as in the proof of Lemma
5.17
and get

\left|v_{av}(t_{0}^{\prime})-v_{av}(t_{0})\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\tau\in I_{y_{n}}(s)}v_{av}(\tau)+C(\gamma\nu)^{p}.

The rest of the proof goes the same as Lemma
5.17
.
∎

Corollary 5.90
.

Let
w
be defined as in (
5.86
),
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
and fix
a>0
.
Associated with these, let
b_{0},\gamma_{0}
be as in Lemma
5.87
. Then there exists a finite
constant
C=C(n,p,a)>0
with the property that for any
\nu>0
,
b\geq b_{0}
,
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0}]
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and any point
(x,t)\in\left\{(x,t):N_{a}(w)(x,t)>\nu\right\}

such that
S_{p,b}(H)+\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(\nabla u)(x,t)+\delta(\varepsilon,A)N_{b}(\nabla u)\leq\gamma\nu
,
where
\delta(\varepsilon,A):=\varepsilon^{-1/(p(p-1))}\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/(p-1)}+\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2/p}+\left\||\partial_{t}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}+\left\||\nabla^{2}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/p}
, one has

(M_{\hbar_{\nu,a}}w)\big(x,\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t),t\big)\geq\,C\nu,

where
M_{\hbar_{\nu,a}}
is the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function on the graph of
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)
.

The following lemma is the analog of Lemma
5.28
and Lemma
5.75
.

Lemma 5.91
.

Fix
p
such that
p/2
is an even positive integer.
Let
\Omega={\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
and let
{\mathcal{L}}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
be a parabolic operator.
Let
u
be an energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
and let
H=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{nj}\partial_{j}u
. For a fixed (sufficiently large)
a>0
determined below,, consider an arbitrary function
\hbar:{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that it satisfies
the estimates (
5.14
), (
5.15
) and
\hbar\geq 0
.
Then
we have the following:

For any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0

such that if

\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
and

\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

then
for all arbitrary parabolic surface balls
\Delta_{r}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
of radius
r
such that at least one point of
\Delta_{r}

satisfies
\hbar(x,t)\leq 2r
, we have the following estimate for an arbitrary
c\geq 0
:

(5.92)

\int_{1/6}^{6}\int_{\Delta_{r}}\big|H^{p/2}\big(x,\theta\hbar(x,t),t\big)-c\big|^{2}\,dx\,dt\,d\theta\leq C(1+\varepsilon^{-1})\iint_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla H\right|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx\\
+\varepsilon\left(\|\tilde{N}_{2,a}\left((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\right)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{N}_{a}(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\right)\\
+\varepsilon\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}dX\,dt+\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}\,dX\,dt,

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
that only depends on
a,\Lambda,n
but not on
k
,
u_{k}
,
c
,
\varepsilon
or
\Delta_{r}
.
Here,

\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar):=\left\{(x,x_{n},t):\,(x,t)\in\Delta_{2r}\text{ and }\frac{\hbar(x,t)}{12}<x_{n}<18r\right\},

where

\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)\cap\left\{(x,x_{n},t):x_{n}>\varepsilon r\right\}
.
The cones used to define the nontangential
maximal functions in this lemma have vertices on
\partial\Omega
.

Proof.

We need to control the term

II=2\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)\partial_{nn}^{2}(H^{p/2}-c)x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt.

We write

\displaystyle\partial_{nn}^{2}(H^{p/2})

\displaystyle=\divg\left(B\nabla(H^{p/2})\right)-\sum_{(i,j)\neq(n,n)}\partial_{i}(b_{ij}\partial_{j}(H^{p/2}))

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(H^{p/2})+\frac{1}{a_{nn}}\partial_{t}(H^{p/2})-\sum_{(i,j)\neq(n,n)}\partial_{i}(b_{ij}\partial_{j}(H^{p/2})),

where
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}=-a_{nn}^{-1}\partial_{t}+\divg(B\nabla\cdot)
. We have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(H^{p/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{2}H^{p/2-1}\left[-a_{nn}^{-1}\partial_{t}H+\divg(B\nabla H)\right]+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)H^{p/2-2}B\nabla H\cdot\nabla H

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{2}H^{p/2-1}T+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)H^{p/2-2}B\nabla H\cdot\nabla H,

by (
4.5
). So the new term that we need to consider is

II_{new}=p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)H^{p/2-1}Tx_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

which is the sum of the following 4 terms:

J_{1}:=p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)H^{p/2-1}(\partial_{i}b_{in})\partial_{t}u\,x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{2}:=-p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)H^{p/2-1}(\partial_{t}b_{in})\partial_{i}u\,x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{3}:=-p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)H^{p/2-1}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})\,H\,x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{4}:=-p\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)H^{p/2-1}\sum_{j<n}[\partial_{i}(b_{ij}\partial_{j}(a_{nk}\partial_{k}u))-\partial_{k}(b_{kn}\partial_{j}(a_{ji}\partial_{i}u))]\,x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt.

For the term
J_{1}
, we use the PDE for
u
to get that
|\partial_{t}u|\sim|\nabla A||\nabla u|+|\nabla^{2}u|
. So by Cauchy-Schwarz,

|J_{1}|\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla B\right|^{2}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\left[\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\nabla A|^{2}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\right]\\
\leq C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p/2}\\
+C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}\left\|N(H{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{(p-2)/2}\\
\cdot\left(\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}dx_{n}dx\,dt\right)^{1/p}.

By Young’s inequality and the observation that
|H|\lesssim|\nabla u|
, we get that

|J_{1}|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}\|N\left((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+\varepsilon\|{N}_{a}(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}\\
+\varepsilon\int_{2\Delta_{r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}dX\,dt.

Next we estimate the term
J_{4}
. Using the product rule, we get terms from the bracket that can be bounded by
|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|
and
|\nabla A||\nabla^{2}u|
, and thus can be treated as
J_{1}
. When 2 derivatives fall on the coefficients
A
, one of them is always in
x_{j}
for
j<n
. For these terms, we integrate by parts in
x_{j}
to move
\partial_{j}
away from the coefficients
a_{ji}
and
a_{nk}
. This process generates terms that can be bounded by (up to a multiplicative constant) the sum of the following terms:

\displaystyle J_{41}

\displaystyle:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{j}(H^{p/2})||H|^{p/2-1}|\nabla A||\nabla u|x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{2}\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{j}H||H|^{p-2}|\nabla A||\nabla u|x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

\displaystyle J_{42}

\displaystyle:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||\partial_{j}(H^{p/2-1})||\nabla A||\nabla u|x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||\partial_{j}H||H|^{p/2-2}|\nabla A||\nabla u|x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{43}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{44}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\nabla A||\nabla\partial_{j}u|x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{45}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\nabla A||\nabla u|x_{n}\,\partial_{j}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

and the boundary term

J_{4b}:=\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\nabla A||\nabla u|\,x_{n}\zeta\,|\nu_{j}|\,dS.

As before, we use Cauchy-Schwarz for all these terms. For
J_{41}
, we let
|\partial_{j}H|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}\zeta
be one integrand and
|\nabla A|^{2}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\zeta
be the other. The latter is further bounded by
|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}x_{n}\zeta
. So

J_{41}\leq\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla H\right|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx+C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}.

For
J_{42}
, we get
|\partial_{j}H|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}\zeta
as one integrand and
|\nabla A|^{2}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}|H|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\zeta
as the other. The latter is further bounded by
|\nabla A|^{2}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}x_{n}\zeta
. This gives

J_{42}\leq\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\nabla H\right|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}\,dx_{n}dtdx+C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}.

For
J_{43}
, we put
|\nabla A|^{2}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}x_{n}\zeta
as one integrand and
|\nabla A|^{2}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\zeta
as the other. The latter is bounded by
|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}x_{n}\zeta
. So

J_{43}\leq C\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}\right).

J_{44}
can be estimated the same as the term involving
\nabla^{2}
from
J_{1}
. For
J_{45}
, we put
|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}x_{n}|\partial_{j}\zeta|^{2}
in the first integral and
|\nabla A|^{2}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\zeta
in the second. The second one is further bounded by
|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}x_{n}\zeta
, while the first one can be estimated similarly as in (
5.42
), using the bound
r|\partial_{j}\zeta|\leq C
. By Young’s inequality, we get

J_{45}\leq\varepsilon\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}.

The term
J_{4b}
is the same as the boundary term
J_{2b}
in (
5.79
), with
u_{k}
replaced by
H
. Note that we have taken care of all the terms coming from
J_{4}
, as there are no terms involving 3 derivatives on
u
from the bracket in
J_{4}
.

The terms
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
need more work, as we get a
t
derivative in the coefficients, so we are lacking
x_{n}
. Therefore, we write
x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})
in
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
, and then integrate by parts in
x_{n}
. For the term
J_{2}
, this process
gives terms that can be controlled by the sum of 6 terms:
J_{21}
-
J_{25}
and
J_{2b}
. The terms
J_{21}
,
J_{22}
,
J_{24}
,
J_{25}
and
J_{2b}
correspond to the terms
J_{41}
,
J_{42}
,
J_{44}
,
J_{45}
and
J_{4b}
, with
\partial_{j}
replaced by
\partial_{n}
,
|\nabla A|
replaced by
|\partial_{t}A|x_{n}
, and
\nabla u
replaced with
\nabla_{T}u
, and additionally for the boundary term,
\nu_{j}
replaced by
\nu_{n}
. Since
|\nabla A|
and
|\partial_{t}A|x_{n}
satisfy the same Carleson condition, these terms can be bounded the same as the corresponding terms from
J_{4}
, replacing
\left\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}
with
\left\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\right\|_{C}
. We are left with

J_{23}:=\left|\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}(H^{p/2}-c)H^{p/2-1}(\partial_{t}\partial_{n}b_{in})\partial_{i}u\,x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dX\,dt\right|.

To estimate
J_{23}
, we integrate by parts in
t
to move
\partial_{t}
away from
\partial_{n}b_{in}
. This gives
J_{23}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{4}J_{23k}+J_{23b}
, where

J_{231}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}H||H|^{p-2}|\partial_{n}A||\nabla_{T}u|x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{232}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}H||H|^{p/2-2}|H^{p/2}-c||\partial_{n}A||\nabla_{T}u|x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{233}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\partial_{n}A||\partial_{t}\nabla_{T}u|x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,dX\,dt,

J_{234}:=\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\partial_{n}A||\nabla_{T}u|x_{n}^{2}\,|\partial_{t}\zeta|\,dX\,dt,

and

J_{23b}:=\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c||H|^{p/2-1}|\partial_{n}A||\nabla_{T}u|\,x_{n}^{2}\zeta\,|\nu_{t}|\,dS.

For
J_{231}
, we get by Cauchy-Schwarz and
|H|\lesssim|\nabla u|
that

J_{231}\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}H|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}^{3}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{n}A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

We need to control

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}H|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}^{3}dX\,dt.

Observe that by the product rule,

|\partial_{t}H|^{2}\lesssim|\partial_{t}A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{t}(\nabla u)|^{2},

which gives that

(5.93)

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}H|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}^{3}dX\,dt\lesssim\int_{\Delta_{2r}}N_{a}(H)^{p-2}(x,t)\\
\cdot\left[\iint_{\gamma_{a/2}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}y_{n}^{-n+2}dYds+A_{a/2}\left(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)^{2}(x,t)\right]dx\,dt,

where
A_{a/2}\left(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{E}\right)
is given as in (
5.80
).
By the Caccioppoli inequality, or the argument in
[
14
, Section 6]
, the term in the bracket is bounded by

S^{2}_{2,a}(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})(x,t)+S^{2}_{A}(u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})(x,t),

where
S_{2,a}(u\,{\mathds{1}}_{E})
is the square function with aperture
a
of
u
restricting to the set
E
, and

S_{A}(u\,{\mathds{1}}_{E})(x,t):=\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap E}\left(|\partial_{t}A|^{2}y_{n}^{2}+|\nabla A|^{2}\right)|\nabla u|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{1/2}.

By a good-
\lambda
argument as in
[
14
, Section 6]
, one has that

\left\|S_{A}(u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{q}}\leq C\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}^{1/2}\left\|N(\nabla u{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{q}}\quad\text{for all }q>1.

Therefore, from applying Hölder’s inequality to (
5.93
), it follows that

(5.94)

\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{t}H|^{2}|H|^{p-2}x_{n}^{3}\,dX\,dt\\
\leq C\left\|N(H{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p-2}\left(\left\|S_{2,a}(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{2}+\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\right\|_{L^{p}}^{2}\right)\\
\leq C\left\|N((\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}+C\left\|S_{2,a}(\nabla u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Delta_{2r})}^{p}.

This implies that

J_{231}\leq\varepsilon\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}dx_{n}\,dx\,dt\\
+C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\partial_{n}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N((\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

The term
J_{232}
is similar. Using (
5.94
) and Cauchy-Schwarz, one has

J_{232}\leq\varepsilon\int_{\Delta_{2r}}\left(\iint_{\gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\right)^{p/2}dx_{n}\,dx\,dt\\
+\varepsilon\left\|N((\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(\Delta_{r},\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\partial_{n}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

For
J_{233}
, we write

J_{233}\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{n}A|^{2}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}x_{n}\zeta\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla_{T}\partial_{t}u|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\zeta\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

Note that the second integral on the right-hand side can be estimated as (
5.93
), using
A_{a/2}\left(\nabla_{T}u\,{\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\right)
. We thus get
J_{233}
can be bounded by the same quantity as
J_{232}
.
For
J_{234}
, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

J_{234}\lesssim\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}|\partial_{t}\zeta|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}|\partial_{n}A|^{2}|H|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}.

The second term on the right-hand side is bounded by
\left\||\partial_{n}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}\left\|N((\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p/2}
.
The first integral could be estimated similarly as the term
II_{9}
in (
5.44
), but since it can be multiplied by
\varepsilon
after applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can simply use the bound
|\partial_{t}\zeta|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\lesssim r^{-1}
and control
|H^{p/2}-c|
pointwise by its nontangential maximal function. Therefore, we have

J_{234}\leq\varepsilon\left\|N((H^{p/2}-c){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\||\partial_{n}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N((\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

For the boundary term
J_{23b}
, we use Cauchy-Schwarz to get

J_{23b}\leq\frac{1}{10}\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}x_{n}|\nu_{t}|dS+C\int_{\partial\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\theta\hbar)}|\partial_{n}A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}x_{n}^{3}|\nu_{t}|dS.

Using the bound
|\nu_{t}|dS\leq\frac{a^{-2}}{2x_{n}}\,dx\,dt
, we see that the first term on the right-hand side is bounded by
\frac{1}{10}\mathcal{I}
, and thus can be hidden on the left-hand side of (
5.92
). The second term can be estimated by integrating in
\theta
over
[1/6,6]
, and the resulting integral is bounded by

C\left\||\partial_{n}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\left\|N((\nabla u){\mathds{1}}_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

We have completed the estimate for
II_{new}
.
Notice that while in the proof of Lemma
5.75
we have to modify the way we estimate
\iint_{\mathcal{S}(q,\tau,r,r_{0},\frac{1}{6}\hbar)}\left|\partial_{t}(u_{k}^{p/2})\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\,dx_{n}\,dx\,dt
from (
5.49
), in the current setting this term (replacing
u_{k}
by
H
) is exactly (
5.94
). So we are done with the modifications.
∎

Lemma 5.95
.

Fix
p
such that
p/2
is a positive even integer. Let
\mathcal{L}
be an operator as in Lemma
5.91
. Fix
a>0
sufficiently large as in Lemma
5.91
.
There exists
\kappa\in(0,1)
that depends only on
n
, constants
b>a
and
\gamma_{0}
that depend on
a
, and
C>0
that depends only on
n
and the ellipticity constants such that the following holds.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, if
\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\left\||\nabla A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\||\nabla^{2}A|x_{n}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}A|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is sufficiently small (depending on
\varepsilon
), then for any energy solution
u
to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
, for any
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0})
,
\beta>0
, and for
H=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{nj}\partial_{j}u
, there holds

\left|E_{1,\beta}\right|\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:M(\tilde{N}_{2,a}(H^{p/2}))(x,t)>\kappa\beta\right\}\right|.

where

E_{1,\beta}:=\Big\{(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,\tilde{N}_{2,a}(H^{p/2})>\beta,\\
\varepsilon^{-1/p}S_{p,b}(H)+{\varepsilon^{1/p}S_{2,b}(\nabla u)}\leq(\gamma\beta)^{2/p},\varepsilon^{1/p}\tilde{N}_{2,b}(\nabla u)<(\gamma\beta)^{2/p}\Big\}.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to that of Lemma
5.82
or Lemma
5.50
. To estimate
\frac{1}{r_{i}}\iint_{\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}}|H^{p/2}-c|^{2}
, we need to use the PDE of
\partial_{t}H
. This can be handled by an argument similar to that in the proof of Lemma
5.87
, and we omit the details.
∎

Theorem
5.84
then follows from Lemma
5.95
by a standard argument similar to that at the end of Section
5.1
.

6.
Bounds of the
p
-adapted square functions

In this section, we establish key estimates for several variants of the

p
-adapted square functions, which in turn provide bounds for the nontangential maximal function of the gradient of the solution. It provides a parabolic analog of the results in
[
19
, Section 6]
, but the arguments differ significantly from the elliptic setting in many places.

We start with the following lemma, which is a parabolic analog of
[
19
, Lemma 6.1]
. In the elliptic setting, one can start directly with bounding
\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}
by
\|\nabla_{T}u\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}
using the solvability of the
L^{p}
Regularity problem. In the parabolic setting, however, this strategy would produce an extra term
\|D_{t}^{1/2}u\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}
, which cannot be controlled (at least easily) by a
p
-adapted square function or its variants. Therefore, we start instead from the ‘
N<S_{p}
’ estimate (Theorem
5.65
), then derive an appropriate estimate on the
p
-adapted function.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
p=2
or
p=4m
, for some
m=1,2,\dots
and
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Let
A
be as in Theorem
5.65
, and
let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
. Then there exists
\delta>0

such that if

\||\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\|_{C}+\left\||\nabla_{T}A|x_{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\|_{C}+\||\partial_{t}a_{nn}|x_{n}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\delta,

then for some

K=K(\lambda,\Lambda,n,p)>0
we have for an energy solution
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
the estimate:

(6.2)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}N^{p}(\nabla_{T}u)\,dx\,dt\leq K\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+K\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}\left[N^{p}(\nabla u)+S^{p}(\nabla u)\right]\,dx\,dt.

Here
H=\sum_{j}a_{nj}\partial_{j}u
, and in particular
H\big|_{\partial\Omega}=A\nabla u\cdot\nu\big|_{\partial\Omega}
is the Neumann data on
\partial\Omega
.

Proof.

We apply first the results of Section
5
to the components of the tangential gradients

\nabla_{T}u
. It follows that for all sufficiently small
\delta=\delta(p,k,\lambda,\Lambda)>0
we have by Theorem
5.65
for
p=4m
,
m\in\mathbb{N}

(6.3)

\|N(\nabla_{T}u)\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}^{p}\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\iint_{\Omega}|\partial_{k}u|^{p-2}|\nabla\partial_{k}u|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}\left[N^{p}(\nabla u)+S^{p}(\nabla u)\right]\,dx\,dt.

This claim also holds when
p=2
with the usual square function.
The second term on the right-hand side of (
6.3
) already has the desired form, we work further on the first term. From here up to (
6.11
), the calculation proceeds as in
[
14
, Lemma 8.5]
but with some further modifications as in our case
p\neq 2
and also the matrix
A
is not of the block-form.

We start with a local estimate on
(0,r)\times Q_{r}
, where
Q_{r}=Q_{r}(y,s)
is a parabolic cube on the boundary as defined earlier. Let
\zeta=\zeta(x,t)
be a smooth cutoff function that satisfies

\zeta=\begin{cases}1\quad\text{in }Q_{r},\\
0\quad\text{outside }Q_{2r},\end{cases}

and that for some constant
0<c<\infty

r\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta\right|+r^{2}\left|\partial_{t}\zeta\right|\leq c\quad\text{where }1\leq i\leq n-1.

To lighten notation, we denote
w_{k}:=\partial_{k}u
for
1\leq k\leq n
. Fix a
k\in\left\{1,2,\dots,n-1\right\}
, then
we can write using the ellipticity condition and the fact that
|w_{k}|^{p-2}=w_{k}^{p-2}
as
p-2
is even

(6.4)

\lambda\Lambda^{-1}(p-1)\iint_{Q_{r}\times(0,r)}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla w_{k}|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq\\
\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla(w_{k}^{p-1})(\zeta^{2}x_{n})dX\,dt=\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg\left(w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}a_{nn}^{-1}A\nabla w_{k}\right)\\
-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{p-1}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla\left(a_{nn}^{-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w_{k}^{p-1}a_{nn}^{-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}).

We then use the divergence theorem for the first term on the right-hand side, which gives us a boundary term

(6.5)

\frac{r}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)(x,r,t)\zeta(x,t)^{2}\,dx\,dt+\frac{r}{p}\sum_{j<n}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}\partial_{j}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)(x,r,t)\zeta(x,t)^{2}\,dx\,dt.

For the second term, we write

\nabla\left(a_{nn}^{-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)=\nabla(a_{nn}^{-1})\zeta^{2}x_{n}+\nabla(\zeta^{2})a_{nn}^{-1}x_{n}+\zeta^{2}a_{nn}^{-1}\nabla x_{n}
, which gives three terms correspondingly. Notice that
a_{nn}^{-1}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla x_{n}=\partial_{n}w_{k}+\sum_{j<n}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}\partial_{j}w_{k}
, and so the corresponding term in the integral
for the leading term

-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{p-1}\partial_{n}w_{k}\zeta^{2}dX\,dt=-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{p}\zeta^{2}\right)dX\,dt\\
=\frac{1}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}\zeta(x,t)^{p}dx\,dt-\frac{1}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{p}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dx\,dt,

while the terms
j<n
can be further written as (after two integrations by parts):

(6.6)

-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{p-1}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}\partial_{j}w_{k}\zeta^{2}dX\,dt=-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}\partial_{j}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)\zeta^{2}(\partial_{n}x_{n})dX\,dt\\
=\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{n}(a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj})\partial_{j}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)\zeta^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{j}(a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj})\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)\zeta^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-\frac{1}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}\partial_{j}(w_{k}^{p})\zeta^{2}r\,dx\,dt-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)\partial_{j}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

The first term of the last line above cancels part of the terms from the boundary term (
6.5
). To summarize, the right-hand side of (
6.4
) is equal to

(6.7)

\frac{r}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)(x,r,t)\zeta(x,t)^{2}dx\,dt+\frac{1}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dx\,dt\\
-\frac{1}{p}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{p}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dx\,dt-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{p-1}a_{nn}^{-1}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla_{x}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{p-1}a_{nn}^{-1}a_{nj}(\partial_{n}w_{k})\partial_{j}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}\,dX\,dt+C-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n})\,dX\,dt.

Here by
C
we have denoted terms that enjoy a Carleson bound (the two terms in the second line of (
6.6
) and the term arising from
\nabla(a_{nn}^{-1})\zeta^{2}x_{n}
) which are of the form

|C|\lesssim\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{p-1}|\nabla w_{k}||\nabla A|\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt.

Using Cauchy-Schwarz, AG-inequality and the Carleson condition for
\nabla A
this is further bounded by

|C|\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla w_{k}|^{2}(\zeta^{2}x_{n})dX\,dt+C_{\varepsilon}\|\mu\|_{C}\int_{Q_{2r}}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt.

Here we choose
\varepsilon>0
such that we can hide the first term on the right-hand side into the first term of
the second line of (
6.4
). The last term above is as required in Theorem
5.6
, provided

\|\mu\|_{C}<\delta
is sufficiently small.

Now we use the PDE of
w_{k}
to treat the last term of (
6.7
). Since
\divg A\nabla u=\partial_{t}u
, a direct computation shows that

-\divg A\nabla w_{k}=-\partial_{t}w_{k}+\divg\left((\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\right),

where we have used the notation
\vec{w}:=(w_{1},w_{2},\cdots,w_{n})^{T}=\nabla u
. Therefore,

-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n})dX\,dt=-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\partial_{t}w_{k}(w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n})dX\,dt\\
+\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\divg\left((\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\right)w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

We continue to compute

I_{1}=-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{t}\left(a_{nn}^{-1}w_{k}^{p}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)dX\,dt\\
+\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}w_{k}^{p}\partial_{t}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}dX\,dt+\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})w_{k}^{p}\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt\\
=\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}w_{k}^{p}\partial_{t}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}dX\,dt+\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})w_{k}^{p}\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt,

since the first term equals to zero. For term
I_{2}
we have

I_{2}=\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}(\partial_{k}A)(\divg\vec{w})w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}(\partial_{k}a_{nn}^{-1})\nabla A\cdot\vec{w}w_{k}^{p-1}\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt\\
-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\nabla A\cdot\partial_{k}(\vec{w}w_{k}^{p-1})\zeta^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\nabla A\cdot\vec{w}w_{k}^{p-1}\partial_{k}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}dX\,dt.

Notice, with the exception of the last term we may again using the Cauchy-Schwarz estimate, Carleson condition and AG inequality to write

I_{2}=D-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}a_{nn}^{-1}\nabla A\cdot\vec{w}w_{k}^{p-1}\partial_{k}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}dX\,dt,

where

|D|\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla w_{k}|^{2}(\zeta^{2}x_{n})dX\,dt+C_{\varepsilon}\|\mu\|_{C}\int_{Q_{2r}}[N(\nabla u)^{p}+S(\nabla u)^{p}]dx\,dt.

Indeed, we have

(6.8)

\iint_{2Q\times[0,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p-1}|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla A|x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
\leq\left(\iint_{2Q\times[0,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p-2}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{2Q\times[0,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}|\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\left(\int_{2Q}N(\nabla u)^{p-2}\iint_{\Gamma(x,t)}|\nabla^{2}u(X)|^{2}{x_{n}}^{-n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{2Q\times[0,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}|\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\left(\int_{2Q}N(\nabla u)^{p-2}S^{2}(\nabla u)\,dx\right)^{1/2}\|\mu\|_{C}^{1/2}\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p/2}_{L^{p}(2Q)}\\
\leq C\|\mu\|_{C}\left[\|S(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}+\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}\right],

with analogous estimates for other terms.

We now use (
6.7
) and our computation of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
to obtain a global estimate on
(0,r)\times\partial\Omega
.

Let
\left\{Q_{2r}^{\ell}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
be a collection of disjoint parabolic cubes of sidelength
2r
that covers
\partial\Omega
. Let
\left\{\zeta_{\ell}^{2}\right\}_{\ell}
be a partition of unity subordinate to this collection, that is,
\supp\zeta_{\ell}\subset Q_{2r}^{\ell}
and
\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\zeta_{\ell}^{2}\equiv 1
on
\partial\Omega
. Moreover, for any
\ell
,

\zeta_{\ell}=1
in
Q^{\ell}_{r}
,
and for some constant
0<c<\infty

r\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta_{\ell}\right|+r^{2}\left|\partial_{t}\zeta_{\ell}\right|\leq c\quad\text{where }1\leq i\leq n-1.

Note that

(6.9)

\sum_{\ell}\partial_{x_{i}}\left(\zeta_{\ell}^{2}\right)=0\quad\text{for }1\leq i\leq n,\quad\text{and }\sum_{\ell}\partial_{t}\left(\zeta_{\ell}^{2}\right)=0.

By (
6.7
), the computation of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, and (
6.9
), we can write for each
k<n

(6.10)

C(\lambda,\Lambda,p)\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla w_{k}|^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt\\
\leq\frac{r}{p}\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{p}\right)(x,r,t)dx\,dt+\frac{1}{p}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}dx\,dt-\frac{1}{p}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{p}dx\,dt\\
+C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}[N(\nabla u)^{p}+S(\nabla u)^{p}]dx\,dt\underbrace{+\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})w_{k}^{p}x_{n}\,dX\,dt}_{J}.

The last term
J
requires more work. We introduce
x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})
and further integrate by parts. It follows that

\left|J\right|=\underbrace{\frac{1}{2p}\left|\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{t}(a_{nn}^{-1})w_{k}^{p}(x,r,t)r^{2}\,dx\,dt\right|}_{=:J_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\partial_{t}A|w_{k}^{p-1}|\nabla w_{k}|\,x_{n}^{2}\,dX\,dt}_{:=J_{2}}\\
+\left|\frac{1}{2p}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{t}\partial_{n}(a_{nn}^{-1})w_{k}^{p}x_{n}^{2}\,dX\,dt\right|\leq J_{1}+J_{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\partial_{n}(a_{nn}^{-1})||w_{k}|^{p-1}|\partial_{t}w_{k}|x_{n}^{2}dX\,dt}.

We denote the last term
J_{3}
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz, the Carleson conditions for
\partial_{t}A
and
\nabla A

and AG-inequality we obtain the following bounds for
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
:

|J_{2}|+|J_{3}|\leq\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}[S(\nabla u)^{p}+A(\nabla u)^{p}]dx\,dt+C_{\delta}\|\mu\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt.

By (
2.20
), these terms are like on the right-hand side of (
6.2
). For the term
J_{1}
we observe that since
|\partial_{t}A|r^{2}\leq\delta^{1/2}
, this term is small and can be bounded by
C\delta^{1/2}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}^{p}(x,r,t)dx\,dt
.

Fix any
r_{0}>0
. We integrate (
6.10
) in
r
variable over
[0,r_{0}]
and divide both sides by
r_{0}
. Since
(\partial_{n}w^{p})x_{n}=\partial_{n}(w^{p}x_{n})-w^{p}
,

\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}r\partial_{n}(w_{k}^{p})(x,r,t)dx\,dt\,dr=r_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r_{0},t)^{p}dx\,dt-\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}^{p}(x,x_{n},t)dX\,dt,

and hence

C(\lambda,\Lambda,p)\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left(x_{n}-\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{r_{0}}\right)|w_{k}|^{p-2}\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}dX\,dt\\
\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r_{0},t)^{p}dx\,dt+\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}dx\,dt-\frac{2-C\delta^{1/2}}{r_{0}}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,x_{n},t)^{p}dX\,dt\\
+C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}[N(\nabla u)^{p}+S(\nabla u)^{p}]dx\,dt.

Truncating the integral on the left-hand side to
[0,\frac{r_{0}}{2}]
and summing over all
k<n
we obtain for all small
\delta>0
after we let
r_{0}\to\infty
:

\sum_{k<n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\Omega}|w_{k}|^{p-2}\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq C\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}dx\,dt+C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}[N(\nabla u)^{p}+S(\nabla u)^{p}]dx\,dt,

since by the assumed decay of
\nabla u
we have that
\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r_{0},t)^{p}dx\,dt\to 0
as
r_{0}\to\infty
.
Hence by (
6.3
) it follows that

(6.11)

\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{T}u)^{p}dx\,dt\leq C\sum_{k<n}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}dx\,dt+C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}[N(\nabla u)^{p}+S(\nabla u)^{p}]dx\,dt.

It remains to establish estimates for
\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}dx\,dt
. We again localize and work
on one of the sets
\left\{Q_{2r}^{\ell}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
, from the collection of disjoint parabolic cubes of sidelength
2r
that covers
\partial\Omega
. With cutoffs
\zeta_{\ell}
as above such that
(\zeta^{2}_{\ell})_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
is a partition of unity we consider another cutoff function

\eta:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\eta(x_{n})=1
on
[0,r]
,
|\eta^{\prime}|\leq 5/r
and its support contained
in
[0,2r]
. Now let

(6.12)

\xi_{\ell}(X,t)=\xi_{\ell}(x,x_{n},t)=\zeta^{2}_{\ell}(x,t)\eta(x_{n}).

We work on estimating
\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}\xi_{\ell}(x)dx\,dt
in local
coordinates on
2Q_{\ell}\times(0,5r)
. We now drop the dependence on
\ell
(working on a single parabolic cube). For each
k\leq n-1
we have

(6.13)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}|w_{k}(x,0,t)|^{p}\xi(x)dx\,dt=-\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{n}(|w_{k}|^{p}\xi)(X)dX\,dt\\
=-\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}(\partial_{n}w_{k})\xi\,dX\,dt-\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p}\zeta^{2}\eta^{\prime}\,dX\,dt=I+II.

The term
II
is
controlled by
\frac{1}{r}\iint_{K_{Q}}|\nabla u|^{p}
, where
K_{Q}=\{X=(x,x_{n},t);(x,t)\in 2Q\text{ and }r\leq x_{n}\leq 2r\}
. We deal with the term
I
.
Since
\partial_{n}w_{k}=\partial_{k}w_{n}
, by writing

w_{n}=\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}w_{i}-\sum_{i<n}\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}w_{i}=\frac{H}{a_{nn}}-\sum_{i<n}\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}w_{i}

we have

(6.14)

I=-p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}(\partial_{k}w_{n})\xi\,dX\,dt\\
=-p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi\,dX\,dt+p\sum_{i<n}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}w_{i}\right)\xi\,dX\,dt.

The second term of (
6.14
) can be further written as

(6.15)

p\sum_{i<n}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}w_{i}\right)\xi\,dX\,dt\\
=p\sum_{i<n}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}w_{i}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi\,dX\,dt+\sum_{i<n}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{i}(|w_{k}|^{p})\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\xi\,dX\,dt.

We introduce
\partial_{n}x_{n}=1
into both the terms of (
6.15
)
and integrate by parts. This gives

(6.16)

-\sum_{i<n}\left[p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{n}\left(|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}w_{i}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi\right)x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{n}\left(\partial_{i}(|w_{k}|^{p})\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\xi\right)x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right]\\
=-\sum_{i<n}\Bigg[p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{n}(|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k})w_{i}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{i}(|w_{k}|^{p})\partial_{n}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}(\partial_{n}w_{i})\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\\
p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}w_{i}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\zeta^{2}\eta^{\prime}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{i}(|w_{k}|^{p})\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\zeta^{2}\eta^{\prime}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}w_{i}\partial_{n}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}}\partial_{n}\partial_{i}(|w_{k}|^{p})\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\Bigg].

In the last two terms we integrate by parts one more time, moving the
\partial_{k}
or
\partial_{i}
derivatives.
This gives

(6.17)

\sum_{i<n}\Bigg[p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}}\partial_{k}(|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k})w_{i}\partial_{n}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}(\partial_{k}w_{i})\partial_{n}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{n}(|w_{k}|^{p})\partial_{i}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+p\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{+}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}w_{i}\partial_{n}\left(\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}\right)(\partial_{k}\zeta^{2})\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}_{+}}\partial_{n}(|w_{k}|^{p})\frac{a_{ni}}{a_{nn}}(\partial_{i}\zeta^{2})\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\Bigg]

The first three terms on the right-hand side of both (
6.16
) and
(
6.17
) enjoy the same bound as (
6.8
) we have already done above.

The fourth term on right-hand side of (
6.16
) can be estimated
by

(6.18)

C\iint_{2Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}|\nabla A|\textstyle{\frac{x_{n}}{r}}\,dX\,dt\\
\leq\left(\iint_{2Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}{\textstyle\frac{x_{n}}{r^{2}}}\displaystyle\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{2Q\times[0,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}|\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\left(\int_{2Q}N(\nabla u)^{p}(x)\,dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}\|\mu\|_{C}^{1/2}\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p/2}_{L^{p}(2Q)}=\|\mu\|_{C}^{1/2}\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p}_{L^{p}(2Q)}.

The fifth term on right-hand side of (
6.16
) can be estimated
by

(6.19)

C\iint_{2Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p-1}|\nabla^{2}u|\textstyle{\frac{x_{n}}{r}}\,dX\,dt\lesssim\displaystyle r^{n+2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{2Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}\displaystyle\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\times\\
\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{2Q\times[0,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p-2}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\leq\displaystyle r^{n+2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{2Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla u|^{p}\displaystyle\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\times\\
\left(\fint_{2Q}N(\nabla u)dx\,dt\right)^{(p-2)/2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{2Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\frac{C_{\delta}}{r}\iint_{\widetilde{K_{Q}}}|\nabla u|^{p}\,dX\,dt+\delta\int_{2Q}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt.

To get the last line we used some standard parabolic estimates away
from the boundary (such as Caccioppoli inequality for the second gradient which holds when
|\nabla A|r\leq C
). Here and in the sequel,
\widetilde{K_{Q}}
is a small enlargement of
K_{Q}
.

We return now to the first term on the right-hand side of (
6.14
).
By introducing
\partial_{n}x_{n}=1
and integrating by parts, we have

(6.20)

-p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi\,dX\,dt=-p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi(\partial_{n}x_{n})\,dX\,dt\\
=p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\partial_{n}(|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k})\partial_{k}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\zeta^{2}\eta^{\prime}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{n}\partial_{k}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt,

where the last term further yields after moving the
\partial_{k}
derivative:

(6.21)

-p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\partial_{k}(|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k})\partial_{n}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt-p\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}w_{k}\partial_{n}\left(\frac{H}{a_{nn}}\right)(\partial_{k}\zeta^{2})\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

If the derivative in the first two terms on the right-hand side of
(
6.20
) and (
6.21
) falls on
a_{nn}^{-1}
, we obtain terms we have already bounded above (see
(
6.8
) and (
6.18
)). If the derivative falls on
H

the first term on the right-hand side of both (
6.20
) and
(
6.21
) is bounded by

C\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla w_{k}||\nabla H|\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
\leq C\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla w_{k}|^{2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|w_{k}|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\lesssim\left(\int_{2Q}N^{p-2}(w_{k})(x,t)\int_{\Gamma(x,t)}|\nabla w_{k}(Y,s)|^{2}y_{n}^{-n}dYds\,dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
=\left(\int_{2Q}N^{p-2}(w_{k})(x,t)S^{2}(w_{k})(x,t)\,dx\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C_{\delta}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\delta\left[\|N(w_{k})\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}+\|S(w_{k})\|^{p}_{L^{p}(2Q)}\right].

If the derivative falls on
H
in the second term of (
6.20
),
we get terms of the same form as (
6.18
) and (
6.19
).

It follows that for all
k\leq n-1
we have

(6.22)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{p}\zeta^{2}(x,t)dx\,dt\leq C_{\delta}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+\delta\left[\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}+\|S(w_{k})\|^{p}_{L^{p}(2Q)}\right]+\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\widetilde{K_{Q}}}|\nabla u|^{p}\,dX\,dt+E.

Here
E
denotes remainder terms; these are the last two terms of
(
6.17
) and the last term of (
6.21
) when the derivative
falls on
H
. We now sum (
6.22
) over all
k\leq n-1
and
also sum over all coordinate patches
Q_{\ell}
. We notice that the
error terms
E
will completely cancel out as
\sum_{\ell}(\partial_{k}\zeta^{2}_{\ell})=0
. This yields a global estimate for all
r>0:

(6.23)

\int_{\partial\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p}dx\,dt\leq C\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-2}|\nabla H|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+C\delta\left[\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}^{p}+\|S(\nabla_{T}u)\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}\right]+\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\partial\Omega\times(r/2,3r/2)}|\nabla u|^{p}\,dX\,dt.

As before since for the energy solutions

\int_{\partial\Omega}\nabla u(x,r,t)^{p}dx\,dt\to 0
as
r\to\infty
, it follows that the last term converges to zero and hence
by (
6.11
) the desired estimate
(
6.2
) holds.
∎

In the following lemma we again assume that
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 6.24
.

Let
p\geq 2
be an integer,
k
be an integer such that
0\leq k\leq p-2
.
Let
A
be a matrix with bounded measurable coefficients that satisfies the ellipticity condition, the Carleson condition for coefficients matrix
A
. Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
. Then there exists
\delta>0

such that if
\|\mu\|_{C}<\delta
then for some

K=K(\lambda,\Lambda,n,p)>0
we have for an energy solution
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
the estimate:

(6.25)

\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}|\nabla H|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq K\int_{\partial\Omega}|H|^{p}\,dx\,dt\\
+K(p-k-2)\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|H|^{k+1}|\nabla H|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+K\delta\left[\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}^{p}+\|S(\nabla u)\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}\right].

Proof of Lemma
6.24
.
Fix any
k\in\{0,1,2,\dots,p-2\}
.
Let us choose a cutoff function
\xi
as in (
6.12
). We aim to bound

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times(0,r)\times\mathbb{R}}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}|\nabla H|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt,

and then let
r\to\infty
.
Clearly, it suffices to get bounds for

\displaystyle I:=\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}b_{ij}(\partial_{i}H)(\partial_{j}H)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt

for some matrix
B
(to be specified later) satisfying a uniform ellipticity condition and then sum over the decomposition of
\mathbb{R}^{n}=\bigcup_{\ell}Q_{\ell}
.

We integrate
I
by parts. Since
|H|^{k}\partial_{i}H=1/(k+1)\partial_{i}(|H|^{k}H)
, this gives

(6.26)

I=-\frac{1}{k+1}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H\partial_{i}(b_{ij}\partial_{j}H)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-\frac{1}{k+1}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}Hb_{nj}(\partial_{j}H)\xi\,dX\,dt\\
\quad\qquad-\frac{1}{k+1}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}Hb_{nj}(\partial_{j}H)(\partial_{i}\xi)x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-\frac{p-k-2}{k+1}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-4}(\nabla_{T}u\cdot\partial_{i}(\nabla_{T}u))|H|^{k}Hb_{ij}(\partial_{j}H)\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

The second term only appears in (
6.26
) if
i=n
as
\partial_{n}x_{n}=1
.

Consider first the third term of (
6.26
) for
i=n
. As
|\eta^{\prime}|\leq 2/r
and
\eta^{\prime}=0
on
[0,r]
we have that this term is bounded by

(6.27)

\iint_{Q\times[r,2r]}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k+1}|\nabla H|\textstyle\frac{x_{n}}{r}\displaystyle\,dX\,dt\\
\leq\delta\int_{2Q}N^{p}(\nabla u)\,dx\,dt+\frac{C_{\delta}}{r}\iint_{\widetilde{K_{Q}}}|\nabla u|^{p}\,dX\,dt,

since this term is of same type as (
6.19
) we have estimated previously. When
i<n
, the third terms of (
6.26
)
will cancel each other out after we sum over the indices
\ell
in the partition of unity
(Q_{\ell})_{\ell}
.

In order to handle the second term of
(
6.26
) we make a choice for the matrix
B
such that
b_{nn}=1
.
Then the second term of (
6.26
) for
j=n
looks as follows:

(6.28)

-\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}(\partial_{n}|H|^{k+2})\xi\,dX\,dt\\
=-\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}\partial_{n}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k+2}\xi)\,dX\,dt\\
\qquad+\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}\partial_{n}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2})|H|^{k+2}\xi\,dX\,dt\\
+\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k+2}(\partial_{n}\xi)\,dX\,dt.

Here the last term again can be estimated by a solid integral

\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\widetilde{K_{Q}}}|\nabla u|^{p}\,dX\,dt
. The first term is equal to a boundary
integral

(6.29)

\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\int_{\partial\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k+2}\xi\,dX\,dt\leq C_{\delta}\int_{2Q}|H|^{p}dx\,dt\\
+\delta\int_{2Q}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p}dx\,dt\leq C_{\delta}\int_{2Q}|H|^{p}dx\,dt+\delta\int_{2Q}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt.

It remains to deal with the second term of (
6.28
). We
differentiate and change the order of derivatives
\partial_{n}
and

\nabla_{T}
:

(6.30)

\displaystyle\frac{p-k-2}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-4}(\nabla_{T}u\cdot\nabla_{T}\partial_{n}u)|H|^{k+2}\xi\,dX\,dt.

We reintroduce the co-normal derivative
H
as
\partial_{n}u=\frac{H}{a_{nn}}-\sum_{j<n}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}w_{j}
. We also
insert the term
\partial_{n}x_{n}=1
into both integrals. Then we
integrate by parts again in the
\partial_{n}
derivative. When
exactly one derivative falls on the coefficients (either
a_{nn}

or
\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}
) we obtain terms bounded by

(6.31)

\iint_{2Q\times(0,2r)}|\nabla A||\nabla u|^{p-1}|\nabla^{2}u|x_{n}\,dX\,dt

which is the term of type (
6.8
) and is bounded by
\|\mu\|_{C}[\|S(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}+\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}]
.

If both
\partial_{n}
and
\nabla_{T}
derivative fall on
coefficients, there are two possibilities. The first possibility
is that they fall on the same coefficient and so then we do a
further integration by parts in
\nabla_{T}
moving this derivative
on other terms. This again will yield term of type (
6.31
).
The second possibility is that they fall on separate coefficients
and the term has a simple bound using the Carleson condition by

\|\mu\|_{C}\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}^{p}
.

We obtain another error term when

\partial_{n}
falls on
\xi
, however in that case we get terms of
type (
6.27
) we have handled before.

Let us deal with the term
when both derivatives fall on
H
. In that case we have

(6.32)

\displaystyle-\frac{p-k-2}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{2Q\times(0,2r)}\frac{1}{a_{nn}}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-4}(\nabla_{T}u\cdot\nabla_{T}\partial_{n}H)|H|^{k+2}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

We move the
\nabla_{T}
derivative off
\partial_{n}H
. We can get a
term of type (
6.31
) and two terms that can be dominated by

(6.33)

\displaystyle C(p-k-2)\iint_{2Q\times(0,2r)}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-4}|\nabla(\nabla_{T}u)||\nabla H||H|^{k+2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C(p-k-2)\iint_{2Q\times(0,2r)}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|\nabla H|^{2}|H|^{k+1}x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

Also, when
\nabla_{T}
lands on
\xi
we get error terms which will cancel when we sum over all
Q_{\ell}
. Observe also that the last term of (
6.26
) can be controlled by

(6.34)

C(p-k-2)\iint_{\Omega_{2r}}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|\nabla(\nabla_{T}u)||\nabla H||H|^{k+1}x_{n}\,dX\,dt

We now deal with the terms arising from

-\sum_{j<n}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}w_{j}
. Here we write

\nabla_{T}\left(\sum_{j<n}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}w_{j}\right)=\sum_{j<n}\nabla_{T}\left(\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}\right)\partial_{j}u+\sum_{j<n}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{j}(\nabla_{T}u).

The contribution of the first term here, when substituted in (
6.30
), can be dealt with by again introducing the factor
\partial_{n}x_{n}
and integrating by parts. When
\partial_{n}
lands on
\nabla_{T}(a_{nj}/a_{nn})
, we can move the tangential derivatives off by again integrating by parts. All this yields terms of the form we have handled previously (such as (
6.31
)). Substituting the second term in (
6.30
) yields

(6.35)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\sum_{j<n}\iint_{\Omega}\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{j}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2})|H|^{k+2}\xi(\partial_{n}x_{n})\,dX\,dt.

Moving
\partial_{n}
across using integration by parts and if necessary moving
\partial_{j}

we obtain terms bounded by (
6.31
), (
6.33
) or (
6.34
). Thus the analysis of the
second term of (
6.26
) for
j=n
reduces to controlling (
6.33
) and (
6.34
), a task which we will postpone for now. When
j<n
in the second
term of (
6.26
) we again introduce
(\partial_{n}x_{n})
. This
gives

\displaystyle-\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}b_{nj}\partial_{j}(|H|^{k+2})\xi(\partial_{n}x_{n})\,dX\,dt

We integrate by parts. When
\partial_{n}
falls on
|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}
we can dominate such a term by (
6.34
),
when
\partial_{n}
falls on
b_{nj}
we use Cauchy-Schwarz, Carleson condition and
(
6.31
) (i.e., we must choose matrix
B
whose coefficients also satisfy the Carleson condition). If
\partial_{n}
hits

\xi
we get terms which can be bounded by (
6.18
) and (
6.19
). Finally the remaining term
is

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}b_{nj}\partial_{j}\partial_{n}(|H|^{k+2})\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

We integrate by parts again in
\partial_{j}
giving us terms
of type (
6.31
), (
6.33
) and (
6.34
). The only remaining terms
we have not yet bounded are the first term of (
6.26
),
(
6.33
) and (
6.34
). The second term of (
6.33
) is already of
desired form (see right-hand side of (
6.25
)). By the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the first term of (
6.33
) can be bounded by

C(p-k-2)\left(\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|\nabla H|^{2}|H|^{k+1}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\times\\
\left(\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-5}|\nabla(\nabla_{T}u)|^{2}|H|^{k+3}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C(p-k-2)\left(\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|\nabla H|^{2}|H|^{k+1}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\times\\
\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p/2-1}_{L^{p}(2Q)}\|S(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(2Q)}.

The last line can be further bounded by

\delta[\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p}_{L^{p}(2Q)}+\|S(\nabla u)\|^{p}_{L^{p}(2Q)}]+C_{\delta}(p-k-2)^{2}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|\nabla H|^{2}|H|^{k+1}\xi x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

Term (
6.34
) can be dealt with in a very similar fashion. We summarize what we have
so far. After summing these inequalities over all cubes
Q_{\ell}
we have that

(6.36)

\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}(B\nabla H\cdot\nabla H)\,\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
\leq K(p-k-2)\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|H|^{k+1}|\nabla H|^{2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+K\int_{\partial\Omega}|H|^{p}\,dx\,dt+\frac{C}{r}\iint_{\partial\Omega\times(r/2,3r/2)}|\nabla u|^{p}\,dX\,dt\\
-\frac{1}{k+1}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H(\widetilde{L}H)\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

Here
\widetilde{L}H=\mbox{ div}(B\nabla H)
. Clearly, (
6.36
) is the desired estimate
(
6.25
) modulo the last two terms. The penultimate term is harmless since
\int_{\partial\Omega}|\nabla u|^{p}(\cdot,r,\cdot)\to 0
as
r\to\infty
and hence this term gets eliminated in the limit.

Let us consider now the last term of (
6.36
). By the calculation in subsection
4.2
we see that by inserting (
4.5
) into last term of (
6.36
) we obtain several terms. Consider first the terms that do not contain
\partial_{t}
, i.e., those that arise from the second line of (
4.5
). When two derivatives hit one of the coefficients we get terms of the form

(6.37)

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega}\sum_{j<n}[b_{ij}(\partial_{i}\partial_{j}a_{ij})(\partial_{k}u)-b_{kn}(\partial_{k}\partial_{j}a_{ji})(\partial_{i}u)]|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt

and the remaining ones can be bounded by

(6.38)

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{p-1}[|\nabla u||\nabla A||\nabla B|+|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla A||B|+|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla B||A|]\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

The terms in (
6.37
) have two derivatives on coefficients
a_{ij}

however one is
\partial_{j}
and
j<n
. We therefore integrate by
parts in
\partial_{j}
. This yields additional terms, but all are
of the form (
6.38
). However, by an estimate similar to
(
6.31
) we get that all the terms of (
6.38
) are
smaller than
C\|\mu\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}[N^{p}(\nabla u)+S^{p}(\nabla u)]\,dx\,dt
. Hence for
\|\mu\|_{C}<\delta
these terms are as expected.

It remains to consider terms arising from the first line of (
4.5
). We omit the first term containing
\partial_{t}H
, the remaining terms give rise to the following terms

(6.39)

-\frac{1}{2(k+1)}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H\left[(\partial_{i}b_{in})\partial_{t}u+(\partial_{t}a_{nn}^{-1})H-(\partial_{t}b_{in})\partial_{i}u\right]\eta(\partial_{n}x_{n}^{2})dX\,dt\\
=\frac{1}{2(k+1)}\Bigg[\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H\left[(\partial_{i}b_{in})\partial_{t}u+(\partial_{t}a_{nn}^{-1})H-(\partial_{t}b_{in})\partial_{i}u\right]\eta^{\prime}x_{n}^{2}dX\,dt\\
+\iint_{\Omega}\partial_{n}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H)(\partial_{i}b_{in})\partial_{t}u\,\eta x_{n}^{2}dX\,dt+\iint_{\Omega}\partial_{n}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k+2})(\partial_{t}a_{nn}^{-1})\,\eta x_{n}^{2}dX\,dt\\
-\iint_{\Omega}\partial_{n}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H\partial_{i}u)(\partial_{t}b_{in})\,\eta x_{n}^{2}dX\,dt+\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H(\partial_{i}b_{in})\partial^{2}_{tn}u\\
+\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H\left[(\partial^{2}_{in}b_{in})\partial_{t}u+(\partial^{2}_{tn}a_{nn}^{-1})H-(\partial^{2}_{tn}b_{in})\partial_{i}u\right]\eta x_{n}^{2}dX\,dt\Bigg].

We estimate these terms line by line. Starting with the first line on the right-hand side of (
6.39
)
we see that these terms can be estimated using the Carleson condition. For the term
\partial_{t}u

we use
|\partial_{t}u|\leq|\nabla^{2}u|+|\nabla A||\nabla u|
. For
\nabla^{2}u
we then again use Caccioppoli inequality for the second gradient. This yields estimates similar to (
6.18
)-(
6.19
):

|\mbox{1st line of \eqref{eq3.44}}|\leq\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt+\frac{C_{\delta}}{r}\iint_{\partial\Omega\times(r/2,3r/2)}|\nabla u|^{p}dX\,dt.

The terms in the second and third line are one of the following type:

(6.40)

|\mbox{2nd/3rd line of \eqref{eq3.44}}|\leq\iint_{\Omega}\Big[|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p-2}|\nabla A|+|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p-1}\\
+|\nabla A|^{3}|\nabla u|^{p}+|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla u|^{p-1}|\partial_{t}A|+|\nabla u|^{p}|\nabla A||\partial_{t}A|+|\nabla\partial_{t}u||\nabla u|^{p-1}|\nabla A|\Big]\eta x_{n}^{2}\,dX\,dt.

Here for the first term we use
|\nabla A|x_{n}\leq\delta^{1/2}
and write the remaining terms as a product of square and nontangential maximal function, second term is handled by Cauchy-Schwarz and Carleson condition for
|\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}
, the third term using
|\nabla A|x_{n}\leq\delta^{1/2}
and Carleson condition, the fourth using
Cauchy-Schwarz and Carleson condition for
|\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}
and the fifth one using Cauchy-Schwarz and then Carleson condition for both
|\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{3}
and
|\nabla A|^{2}x_{n}
.
The last term of (
6.40
) we bound first by Cauchy-Schwarz and AG-inequality by

(6.41)

C_{\delta}\|\mu\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt+\delta\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla\partial_{t}u|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p-2}x_{n}^{3}\eta\,dX\,dt.

The second term is bounded by

\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla u)^{p-2}A(\nabla u)^{2}dx\,dt
, which can be bounded using
S(\nabla u)
and
N(\nabla u)
by Hölder’s inequality and (
2.20
).
Hence, each term of (
6.40
) contains a small term due to presence of derivatives on A and thus

|\mbox{2nd/3rd line of \eqref{eq3.44}}|\leq C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt+C\delta\int_{\partial\Omega}S(\nabla u)^{p}dx\,dt.

It remains to consider terms in the last line of (
6.39
). Each of the terms has
too many derivatives on one of the coefficients of
A
or
B
. Starting with the first term where we have

\partial^{2}_{in}b_{in}
we have observation that we only need to consider
i<n
since
b_{nn}=1
and thus

\partial^{2}_{nn}b_{nn}=0
. But for
i<n
we may integrate by parts in
\partial_{i}
and obtain terms we have already considered in (
6.39
).

Similarly for the remaining terms on the last line of (
6.39
) we integrate by parts in
\partial_{t}
to remove
\partial^{2}_{tn}
from the coefficients. This means
\partial_{t}
will either fall on
H

(and then we use (
4.11
)) or
\partial_{t}
falls on
\nabla_{T}u
and hence it is as in (
6.41
).
It follows that all terms arising from (
4.5
) have been handled with exception of the first one. To do that, we need to consider the term

(6.42)

-\frac{1}{k+1}\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k}H(\partial_{t}H)\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
=-\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}\partial_{t}(|H|^{k+2})\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
=\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\iint_{\Omega}\left[(\partial_{t}a_{nn}^{-1})|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2}|H|^{k+2}+a_{nn}^{-1}\partial_{t}(|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-2})|H|^{k+2}\right]\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt,

after integration by parts. The first term on the last line is harmless (we introduce
x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})
, integrate by parts in
\partial_{n}
and obtain terms analogous to that of
(
6.39
)). We omit the details.

The second term must vanish when
p-k-2=0
and hence we only consider the case when
q=p-k-2\geq 1
. In a calculation that mimics (
5.34
)

(6.43)

\partial_{t}(|w_{k}|^{q})=\divg(A\nabla(|w_{k}|^{q}))+q|w_{k}|^{q-2}w_{k}\left[\partial_{t}w_{k}-\divg(A\nabla w_{k})\right]\\
-q(q-1)|w_{k}|^{q-2}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}\\
=\divg(A\nabla(|w_{k}|^{q}))-q|w_{k}|^{q-2}w_{k}\divg((\partial_{k}A)\nabla u)-q(q-1)|w_{k}|^{q-2}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k},

where as before
w_{k}=\partial_{k}u
. We have also used the PDE for
w_{k}
. Replacing
|\nabla_{T}u|
by sum of the terms
k<n
containing
|w_{k}|
we now have:

(6.44)

A_{k}+B_{k}+C_{k}:=\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\Bigg[\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}\divg(A\nabla(|w_{k}|^{q}))|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-q\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}|w_{k}|^{q-2}w_{k}\divg((\partial_{k}A)\nabla u)|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-q(q-1)\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}|w_{k}|^{q-2}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\Bigg].

Observe that
C_{k}\leq 0
by ellipticity and it can be dropped, as we are seeking a bound from above. The term
B_{k}
(which we expect to be small) satisfies

B_{k}=C(p,k)\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}\partial_{k}(\divg(A)\nabla u)|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+C(p,k)\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}(\partial_{k}A)\nabla^{2}u|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

Here the second term is bounded by (after using Cauchy-Schwarz).

(6.45)

C\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla u|^{p-2}|\nabla A|\eta x_{n}\leq C\|\mu\|^{1/2}_{C}\|S(\nabla u)\|^{2}\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p-2}.

For the first term we integrate by parts in
\partial_{k}
and obtain terms bounded by

(6.46)

C\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p}\eta x_{n}+C\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u||\nabla u|^{p-2}|\nabla A|\eta x_{n}.

The first term is bounded by
\|\mu\|_{C}\|N(\nabla u)\|^{p}
, while the second term was handled earlier.

Returning to
A_{k}
we consider the divergence in variables
i<n
first. Hence

A_{k}=A_{k,i<n}+A_{k,i=n}.

For
A_{k,i<n}
we integrate by parts in
\partial_{i}
to obtain

A_{k,i<n}=-\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)}\sum_{i<n}\iint_{\Omega}\Bigg[\partial_{i}(a_{nn}^{-1})a_{ij}(\partial_{j}|w_{k}|^{p-k-2})|H|^{k+2}\\
+(k+2)a_{nn}^{-1}a_{ij}(\partial_{j}|w_{k}|^{p-k-2})|H|^{k}H(\partial_{i}H)\Bigg]\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt.

The first term is again a small term as it is of the type (
6.45
) considered earlier. For the last term we use Cauchy-Schwarz to get that this term is bounded from above by

(6.47)

C\left(\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla H|^{2}|H|^{k+1}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla\nabla_{T}u|^{2}|\nabla u|^{p-2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq K_{\delta}\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla_{T}u|^{p-k-3}|H|^{k+1}|\nabla H|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+K\delta\left[\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}^{p}+\|S(\nabla u)\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}\right],

by the AG-inequality. These terms correspond to the right-hand side of (
6.25
) as required.

When
i=n
we notice that

a_{nj}(\partial_{j}|w_{k}|^{p-k-2})=(p-k-2)|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}a_{nj}\left(\partial^{2}_{jk}u\right)=(p-k-2)|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}\big[\partial_{k}(a_{nj}\partial_{j}u)\\
-(\partial_{k}a_{nj})\partial_{j}u\big]=(p-k-2)|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}\left[\partial_{k}H-(\partial_{k}a_{nj})\partial_{j}u\right],

which implies that

(6.48)

A_{k,i=n}=\frac{p-k-2}{(k+1)(k+2)}\Bigg[\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}\partial_{n}(|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}\partial_{k}H)|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
-\iint_{\Omega}a_{nn}^{-1}\partial_{n}[(\partial_{k}a_{nj})|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}w_{j}]|H|^{k+2}\eta x_{n}\,dX\,dt\Bigg].

Here in the first term if the derivative
\partial_{n}
hits
|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}
we get a term that can be bounded by (
6.47
). When it falls on
H
we peel off the
\partial_{k}
derivative from
H
to again get a term like (
6.47
),
or (if the
\partial_{k}
falls on the coefficient) a term like (
6.45
).

Similarly, for the second term of (
6.48
), if
\partial_{n}
falls on
|w_{k}|^{p-k-4}w_{k}w_{j}
we have a term like
(
6.45
). Otherwise we have two derivatives on
\partial^{2}_{nk}(a_{nj})
and, after peeling off
\partial_{k}
,
we obtain terms that look like (
6.45
) or like the first term of (
6.46
). For both types we have already established the
desired bounds. Thus we have shown the claim.
\square

7.
S<N
for
\nabla u
.

The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
be a parabolic operator that satisfies (
1.2
), (
1.3
), and (
1.5
). Let
u
be an energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
.
For any
p\in(0,\infty)
, there is
C>0
such that

\left\|S(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}.

To prove Theorem
7.1
, we first derive a
S<N
estimate on sawtooth domains using cutoff functions as in
[
14
, Section 9]
.
Let
\Psi
be a cutoff function on
\Omega
that satisfies the following properties.

(1)

0\leq\Psi\leq 1
,

(2)

for any
p\in(0,\infty)
, the measure given by the density

(7.2)

\left(\left|\nabla\Psi(X,t)\right|^{p}x_{n}^{p-1}+\left|\partial_{t}\Psi(X,t)\right|^{p}x_{n}^{2p-1}\right)dxdx_{n}dt

is a Carleson measure on
\Omega
.

The key difference from
[
14
, Theorem 9.1]
is that we do not assume that the coefficient matrix of the operator is in block form. To achieve that, we first treat
S(\nabla_{T}u)
, and then
S(H)
, where
H=\sum_{1\leq j\leq n}a_{nj}\partial_{j}u
. Observe that

|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}\lesssim|\nabla(\nabla_{T}u)|^{2}+|\partial_{nn}u|^{2}\lesssim|\nabla(\nabla_{T}u)|^{2}+|\partial_{n}H|^{2}+\left|\partial_{n}A\right|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}

and so

(7.3)

\iint|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\lesssim\iint|\nabla(\nabla_{T}u)|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+\iint|\partial_{n}H|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
+\left\||\partial_{n}A|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N\left(\Psi^{m/2}\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\,dt.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
and
u
be as in Theorem
7.1
. Let
\Psi
be a cutoff function that satisfies the following conditions:

(7.5)

|\nabla\Psi(X,t)|+x_{n}|\partial_{t}\Psi(X,t)|\lesssim x_{n}^{-1}\quad\text{ for }(X,t)\in\Omega,

and the measure
\nu
given by the density

(7.6)

d\nu=\left(\left|\nabla\Psi\right|^{2}x_{n}+\left|\nabla\Psi\right|+\left|\partial_{t}\Psi\right|x_{n}\right)dX\,dt\quad\text{ is a Carleson measure on }\Omega.

Let
m\geq 3
be an integer, and let
k\in\left\{1,\dots,n-1\right\}
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there holds

(7.7)

\iint\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq\varepsilon\iint|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}dX\,dt+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}+\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}\right)\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\nabla u)\right|^{2}dx\,dt.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
and
u
be as in Theorem
7.1
. Let
\Psi
be a cutoff function as in Lemma
7.4
.
Let
m\geq 3
be an integer. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\iint\left|\nabla H\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq\varepsilon\iint|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}dX\,dt+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}+\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}\right)\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\nabla u)\right|^{2}dx\,dt.

Combining Lemma
7.4
and
7.8
with the estimate (
7.3
), one gets that

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+L
be a parabolic operator with matrix
A
and let
u
be an energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
. Let
\Psi
be a cutoff function as in Lemma
7.4
.
Let
m\geq 3
be an integer. Then

\iint\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq C\left(\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}+\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}\right)\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\nabla u)\right|^{2}dx\,dt.

Once we have Lemma
7.9
, Theorem
7.1
follows from a good-
\lambda
inequality (Lemma
7.10
below) using the exact same argument as in
[
14
, Section 9]
. We note that, in the analog of the lemma above, namely
[
14
, Lemma 9.3]
, the condition (
7.5
)
has not been required; however, it follows from the construction of the cutoff function adapted to a sawtooth domain in
[
14
, Definition 9.7]
.

Lemma 7.10

(Good-
\lambda
inequality)
.

For any
\nu>0
and any
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

(7.11)

\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu,\,N_{2a}(\nabla u)(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu\right\}\right|\\
\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{2a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu/2\right\}\right|.

We now prove Lemmas
7.4
and
7.8
.

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

The proof has a similar idea as that of Lemma
6.1
, starting from (
6.4
), and in particular, it might be viewed as the special case when
p=2
. But since in the current setting there is a cutoff function
\Psi
, the proof goes differently in multiple places.

For simplicity, we write
w_{k}=\partial_{k}u
. We start with writing

\iint\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\lesssim\iint\frac{A}{a_{nn}}\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt\\
=\underbrace{-\iint\frac{a_{nj}}{a_{nn}}\partial_{j}w_{k}w_{k}\Psi^{m}dX\,dt}_{:=I}-m\iint\frac{A}{a_{nn}}\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla\Psi\left(\Psi^{m-1}w_{k}x_{n}\right)dX\,dt\\
\underbrace{-\iint\divg\left(\frac{A}{a_{nn}}\nabla w_{k}\right)w_{k}\Psi^{m}x_{n}dX\,dt}_{=:J}

by ellipticity and the divergence theorem. The unlabeled term on the right-hand side can be bounded by the desired quantities using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the assumption that
|\nabla\Psi|^{2}x_{n}dX\,dt
is a Carleson measure:

\left|m\iint\frac{A}{a_{nn}}\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla\Psi\left(\Psi^{m-1}w_{k}x_{n}\right)dX\,dt\right|\\
\leq\varepsilon\iint\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dX\,dt+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}w_{k})\right|^{2}dx\,dt.

For the term
I
